# Тема: крафт

Дословные слова авторов по теме «крафт»: 291 фрагментов из 130 роликов. Читай подряд — это опыт практиков в их собственных словах; «Мысль» и «Для Напарника» — вывод разметчика.



# 🎬 "He Built a Game For His Girlfriend — The New York Times Paid Millions" — Brain & Behavior Explained


### Ручная редактура 12000 слов до 2500 сделала игру доступной всем  ·  [02:14](https://youtu.be/nsg9FZucQts?t=134)
**Мысль:** Одно редакторское решение — оставить только узнаваемые слова — стало одной из главных причин массовости вместо аудитории словарников.

> The original 2013 prototype had about 12,000 five-letter words, including some incredibly obscure ones that nobody had ever heard of. But Pollock herself made this massive editorial contribution. She went through all 12,000 words and manually filtered them down to roughly 2500 everyday commonly recognized words. This single editorial decision is honestly one of the most underrated reasons the game became accessible to the masses instead of just appealing to hardcore dictionary buffs.

**Для Напарника:** Вручную отбирать тексты, названия ориентиров и реплики кота: узнаваемое и тёплое лучше экзотического.



# 🎬 10 UX Patterns Every AI Chat Interface Needs — Kristian


### Прилипание к низу при стриминге, с возможностью прервать слежение и вернуться  ·  [00:46](https://youtu.be/bppkpufBJsI?t=46)
**Мысль:** Самодельная реализация почти всегда неверна; нужны липкость к низу, прокрутка к последнему сообщению при обновлении страницы.

> Number two is to use some kind of library that helps you implement scrolling logic when your AI streams the response. So here in this example if I send this you will see that my cursor will now follow the stream. But at any time I can easily like break this following but then get back to the following of the stream. And I can do it any time and it sticks to the bottom of the stream and continues with the stream. And it's really important because the way you implement it, if you will try to implement it yourself, you will probably get it wrong. So it handles a lot of stuff like the stream stickiness. It also handles that when you first refresh the page, it like scrolls down to the last message, which is also helpful. And you don't need to worry about implementing it. You can just use a library like use stick to bottom. You can just implement this component stick to bottom. stick to bottom content and it will just automatically work.

**Для Напарника:** Лента реплик кота: при стриме ответа держаться низа, но не отнимать управление у пальца.



# 🎬 5 Game Design Secrets That Fix Retention & LTV — Kutovaya Elena


### Полезное трение: IKEA-эффект, но не в основном цикле  ·  [09:46](https://youtu.be/EAfYEhCve6Q?t=586)
**Мысль:** Игры вводят трение намеренно: то, что строишь сам, ценнее. Но трение добавляют только перед бонусом, никогда в core loop; бесполезное трение (формы, навигация) убирают.

> The last one, the fifth one is friction. Usually, friction is any effort your product asks from the user before they get a result. The standard product playbook says cut friction everywhere. Always game design does the opposite. It builds friction in on purpose. No effort, no reward and that means no dopamine hit from overcoming it. Some friction is just bad. For example, I don't know extra form fields, confusing navigation, typing the same thing twice. this kind your conversions, it cost you your day one retention. But some friction is designed on purpose to make the result feel more valuable for the user. Here is a classic example. The EK something you build yourself feels more valuable than the exact same thing handed to you finished completed. Even though it's objectively identical for hot products, here is my advice. Go carefully. First rule, only add friction in front of something extra, something added. A chunky UI for example is just friction with no PO that's a fast track to drop off was sessions was retention was DO but a temporary cash back boost for a bit of extra effort that's a real benefit I guess and the lever on our pool second rule never put friction in your core loop that kills your product it filters people out it ts retention but friction around a gift that's a different that's a chance for the user to invest something of their own in non-game products that this looks like personalizing your setup, building your own profile, building your rowing dashboard, any step where the user puts in a little effort because before they get the result, cutting that kind of friction is not always a win for retention or LTV. Sometimes it just makes the product feel less valuable. The real job isn't to remove all friction. It's to sort it into two piles and only clear out the kind that's useless.

**Для Напарника:** Личная настройка (имя, выбор шага, оформление) даёт чувство своего; но запуск сессии должен быть в один тап, без трения в ядре.

And with that, we can get rid of these links and tuck them into a popover on click and collapse settings and billing and usage together. And finally for these cards, they're feeling a little busy. Let's collapse these buttons into a triple dot menu. Move the date to the center. Collapse these chips to just icons. And move clicks to the right.

> I have a fun story there so we were working on a feature that basically does this right like it looks at a chat it says here are three next steps we think you might want to do the designer working on it s in she brought it to crit she shows it's kind of like the UI the rectangles are like pretty straightforward it's little suggestion chips that appear underneath the chat she showed some examples you're like yeah that's pretty good like that makes sense those are good next steps we should think about you know when and how to roll this out and the crit was sort of tailing off just because it was good like there wasn't a lot to say and then someone was like can you show us the prompt that you used to ask this other instance of Claude to make the right suggestions and she brings up this seven page prompt with like all of these instructions and examples and little tweaks and like the art of prompting is like this Dark Art and she had just she had iterated I think she said like 75 or 100 there's like hundreds of iterations trying different combination of prompts looking at it seeing if it made sense all to get this thing that was just elegant and simple for a user and so this was another sort of like big Awakening moment for me switching into the AI world is prompting is part of the design process and designers need to know how to do it.

# 🎬 7 UI/UX mistakes that SCREAM you’re a beginner — Kole Jain


### Пропуски в пользовательском потоке: поиск, пропуск, скрытые состояния  ·  [00:23](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=23)
**Мысль:** Экран выбора аллергий без поиска и без «пропустить» упускает случаи. Наброски на бумаге ловят дыры, а чаще всего забывают навигацию, скрытые состояния и динамику.

> Number one, user flow. Let's start with something that quietly ruins your UX, user flow. Here's our allergy screen with six preset options, but no search bar if someone's allergic to something else. We also don't have a skip button either. What if the user doesn't have any allergies? These are things you will miss if you don't plan out your flow, but users, they'll feel it instantly. So, even something as simple as sketching boxes on paper helps you catch major gaps early on. And it's not just all about big stuff. Little touches like a filter icon on the search bar or a save button up top go a long way. The most commonly missed elements, navigation links, hidden states, and dynamic interactions like button hovers or micro interactions.

**Для Напарника:** В онбординге Напарника у каждого вопроса должен быть мягкий пропуск («потом»), чтобы никого не загонять.


### Перебор эффектов: меняй цвет тени и блюр, градиент без разброса оттенков  ·  [01:06](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=66)
**Мысль:** Градиенты из разных цветов не работают, градиент допустим только в вариациях одного. Тень лучше смягчать цветом и блюром, и чаще меньше шума значит лучше.

> Early on, I was addicted to visual effects. Shadows, glows, gradients, you name it. But the truth, overusing them can make your designs feel cluttered and amateur. Let's start with the gradient here. It's blue and green, and it's just not working. If you really need a gradient, stick to variations of the same color, like a darker or lighter green. But honestly, this would look way cleaner with no gradient at all. And that's usually the case. Now, let's talk drop shadows for a second. These Figma defaults are way too harsh. Instead of just lowering opacity, I change the entire shadow color, usually to a light gray, and increase the blur significantly. Or once again, remove the shadow completely. Because most of the time, less visual noise equals a better design.

**Для Напарника:** Свечения и тени на острове использовать только там, где есть смысловая привязка, как требует §10 CLAUDE.md.


### Консистентность: единый радиус, одинаковые кнопки, стили и переменные  ·  [02:47](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=167)
**Мысль:** Разные радиусы и одинаковые по смыслу, но по-разному оформленные кнопки выглядят любительски. Помогают стили цвета, переменные размеров и компоненты.

> This one kills me since it's just so easy to fix. You'll notice throughout our design, we have all sorts of different corner radiuses and components like this back and skip button that are exactly the same but designed differently, which just makes the design feel amateur. Instead, let's make a few changes. First, we'll make the two search bars identical other than the prompting text. We'll set the corner radius of all smaller components to be 10 pixels. This is an important one that most designers miss. And of course, we'll update the skip button and back button to match in size, corner radius, and style. Being consistent means using styles for colors, variables for measurements, and components for UI elements to really bring your design together.

**Для Напарника:** Единые радиусы и размеры кнопок «Назад»/«Пропустить» во всех экранах; токены вместо магических чисел.


### Лишние элементы: убирай стрелки там, где работает жест  ·  [04:57](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=297)
**Мысль:** Стрелка при возможности свайпа и обводки без необходимости только визуальный шум.

> Having redundant elements is actually a really common mistake, especially as the trend of having stuff everywhere takes over. Looking at this screen, we can get rid of this arrow since we'll be able to swipe on mobile. And same idea down below. We can also get rid of this arrow since it's really just visual clutter and dim down the color of the button.

**Для Напарника:** Убрать декоративные стрелки там, где работает drag-to-reply и свайп, оставив действие очевидным без украшения.


### Графики не надо переоформлять: ось и читаемые столбцы важнее эстетики  ·  [06:27](https://youtu.be/AH_ugxmLeUM?t=387)
**Мысль:** Закруглённые столбцы без оси и 16 столбцов на 7 дней нечитаемы, а простой график даёт больше пользы.

> Charts are so often messed up by overdesigning the thing that I thought I should make a point to mention it. I have two charts here. Which one do you think I got from Dribble? The one on the right. Yes. And it's basically impossible to read without any vertical axis. And even if we had a vertical axis, it's hard to tell where the bar ends with a rounded top. And then there are 16 bars here, but only 7 days of the week. Why would you do that? Whereas the one on the left is simple, definitely less aesthetic, but conveys so much more useful information to the user.

**Для Напарника:** Если в Мире появится статистика недели, делать её простой и честной, с понятной осью.



# 🎬 8 Point Grid system - Improve your UI designs (Figma file included) — UX Tshili


### Джуниоры гадают размеры; осознанность каждого размера отличает профессионала  ·  [00:22](https://youtu.be/FhGdLUW_8S0?t=22)
**Мысль:** Сетка из 8 точек даёт единую структуру и сохраняет вид после передачи разработчикам.

> I have realized that there is a common theme in people who are more Junior by just getting started in their design career they are not using grids everyone is just guessing they're spacing the size of the elements and the text that every size has to be intentional and this is where the eight point grid system comes in the a point grid is a layout system used in UI design to create consistent visual structure and hierarchy for your designs this ensures that your work is consistent and aligned use the eight point grid system as a guideline to determine the size of elements on your screen and the spacing between them grids allow you to achieve better looking designs faster and they also ensure that your designs will look as polished once you've handed them over to.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое число отступа в компоненте Напарника должно иметь обоснование в шкале.

# 🎬 A Lesson in How Inconsistent UI Kills the First Experience | App Breakdown #88 — App Breakdown


### Длинную анимацию можно показывать, но нужно дать пропустить; триггер — тройной тап  ·  [04:24](https://youtu.be/hgpk0ejGs-E?t=264)
**Мысль:** Автор определял, что человек тапает по экрану три-четыре раза подряд, и без кнопки «пропустить» просто пропускал анимацию. Нетерпение пользователя — сигнал.

> And this animation is taking forever. I am triple tapping and quadruple tapping here to speed it up. So that's one actually pro tip for you is if you want to do a long animation or show a long video to a user, that's totally fine. There's a lot of places where there's valuable, but if they want to skip, they should be able to skip. And one little trick I've used before is not including any skip button or but detecting if users triple or quadruple tap and skipping them in that case.

**Для Напарника:** Хореография RevealIsland и starting→running: тап по экрану во время анимации должен ускорять или завершать её, без отдельной кнопки пропуска.


### Пульсирует, но не нажимается: ложный сигнал аффорданса  ·  [11:02](https://youtu.be/hgpk0ejGs-E?t=662)

> If I tap that, nothing happens. So, this looks like the most tappable thing ever and it's pulsing, but it's not tappable.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает диегетичность из CLAUDE.md: пульсировать/двигаться должно только то, что реально интерактивно или реально живёт в сцене. Проверить, что декоративные движения не выглядят кнопками.



# 🎬 A Redesign Strategy That Works — Manifesto


### Редизайн: сначала структурные изменения по частям, потом визуальный рефреш разом  ·  [05:22](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=322)
**Мысль:** Разделили проблемы на структурные и визуальные: структуру выкатывали инкрементально в старом стиле, чтобы избежать «Франкенштейна», а визуальный рефреш одним релизом без смены рабочих процессов.

> So when I was approaching the execution of a redesign my first instinct was to break it up I was as a product manager coming at this the same way I would come that building a new feature where we would start with an MVP we would a be test it and it Aurra iterate our way to an optimized end state in fact I could not imagine and the alternative which would be you know going heads down for a month working on new designs until this one big reveal moment where we may find that users absolutely hate it but our design team had very different instincts they recognized that the redesign is more than the sum of its parts and they couldn't imagine launching it piecemeal they did not want to put users through this Frankenstein experience where parts of the new style would exist alongside remnants of the old design they just could not stomach it so we wondered if there was a way to get the best of both worlds in order to figure that out we really took a closer look at the specific user pain points that we were trying to solve and we recognized from there that there are actually two sets of issues there are some structural issues things like broken navigation and a lack of hierarchy and then there were also visual issues which were things like outdated button styles and a drab color scheme across all of our platforms making this distinction allowed us to see that we could incrementally launch structural improvements in the old visual style without having to put users through that Frankenstein experience and then once we had made all the structural changes we could launch a visual refresh in one Big Bang but we'd be limiting the risk of user backlash because we wouldn't be forcing users to change their entire workflows overnight.

**Для Напарника:** Не менять сразу и структуру, и вид Напарника; новые ориентиры/фон выкатывать по одному.


### Видеть убывающую отдачу и вовремя переключаться  ·  [11:59](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=719)
**Мысль:** Раз видно влияние каждого куска, понятно, когда изменения становятся мельче и пора идти к более значимой работе.

> Advantage of this approach is really knowing when to move on so because you can see the impact of each individual piece you can recognize when you're starting to hit diminishing returns so as we as the launches went further and further down the line the changes were more subtle and the impacts were more subtle so we knew that it was time to move on to higher impact work.

**Для Напарника:** Полировка Дома/Фокуса имеет предел: фиксировать момент убывающей отдачи, как «done is better than perfect».


### Готово лучше идеального: после 75% списка отдача падает  ·  [14:45](https://youtu.be/iIQmxRA85GQ?t=885)
**Мысль:** Настройки в глубине приложения не стоило доводить до совершенства; решили выпускать.

> But as we got through about 75% of that list we realized that we were hitting diminishing returns we were working on settings dialogs that were hidden deep and corners of the app so we knew that we didn't really want to chase these diminishing returns into perfection and we agreed that done was better than perfect and it was time to launch.

**Для Напарника:** Не полировать глубокие экраны ценой выпуска; приоритет по влиянию.



# 🎬 Aarron Walter — unmatchedstylereview


### Иерархия потребностей интерфейса: функциональность, надёжность, удобство, затем приятность  ·  [02:51](https://youtu.be/ZCcMIchXHP0?t=171)
**Мысль:** Десять лет индустрия решала удобство, а верх пирамиды (радость, запоминаемость, доверие) остался с низкой планкой; именно он создаёт глубину вовлечения.

> So in the talk that i'm doing today and a book that i'm working on now too i talk about this hierarchy of needs which is based on the idea of you know the 1950s maslow's hierarchy of needs which is just a series of strata getting up to the top where you know you've got these base needs and then it enhances to like the self-actualization of forming an identity and relationship in a community and we can apply that to interface design and at the base of that an interface needs to be functional once it's functional it needs to be reliable we can count on it so no fail wheels then it needs to be usable that we can perform the tasks we want to perform and then there's an emotionally engaging a pleasurable aspect that's at the top and the premise of what i've been researching in my work at mailchimp and you know for my book and for my talks and stuff is that top piece that i feel like we've set that bar low that for the past 10 years we've been working really hard just to get things usable which is fantastic that's a noble cause and a very important thing that i'm sure is that our users appreciate that we're heading towards but you know couldn't we make something that's more pleasurable that's memorable that's fun or you know that inspires trust so it's just a deeper engagement level.

**Для Напарника:** После надёжного таймера и чата добавляем приятность: живой фон, дыхание кота, звук костра; это верхний уровень и основной отличитель Напарника.


### Brand persona: спроси не только «кто они», но и «кто мы»; персонаж — каркас для решений  ·  [04:52](https://youtu.be/ZCcMIchXHP0?t=292)
**Мысль:** Описав черты личности бренда (дружелюбный, доверчивый, ворчливый), можно проверять каждую идею, пасхалку или игровую механику на уместность.

> One place that people can start is by creating a brand persona and so we spend a lot of time as user experience designers we do research in the field we research our users try to understand what they need and then we design for that which is great so we're always asking who are they but we never ask who are we and what we're really trying to do as interface designers is we're trying to shift from human computer interaction to human to human interaction where our interface is that layer between the humans that are connecting here so creating a brand persona is flipping that on its head and saying okay so this is the persona of the brand that i'd like to create it has a personality that a product could be a personality or like a person and if that were a person who is it what are its traits it's personality traits is it an aggressive and you know somewhat friendly personality or is it sort of curmudgeon and kind of spiteful personality which may be appropriate in some situations or maybe it's docile and trustworthy so we identify those we create that persona and once we have that it's sort of like a framework on which we can hang our ideas and think about like is this the right thing for this interaction that we're going to design here like if we were going to create an easter egg or we were going to create like some fun discovered experience or maybe a game theory inspired experience is that appropriate for this personality.

**Для Напарника:** Задать для кота письменную персону (тёплый, ненадзирающий, присутствие) и проверять каждую реплику и анимацию на соответствие; согласуется с запретом на стыд.



# 🎬 Accessible Web Design: What Is It & How To Do It — Flux Academy


### Проектируй состояние фокуса для клавиатуры, а не только hover  ·  [07:22](https://youtu.be/-ao_Kc_8rpE?t=442)
**Мысль:** Дизайнеры любят hover, но забывают focus state для тех, кто ходит по сайту через Tab; его не рисуют, потому что сами им не пользуются.

> One more thing that you should care about is focus States so we as designers obviously we like to focus a lot on hover States and you know what happens when people move and hover with the mouse over something create fancy interactions but we a lot of times forget about the focus state so focus States basically is for people who use the keyboard to navigate websites and stuff like that so they might click the tab and then they kind of the selected link is going to be somehow selected right and we need to show how that looks like so this state is called focus State and a lot of designers don't even bother designing it or telling the developers that they should implement this because again this is not something that's visible or used by them and so they forget about it so make sure that you also design for the focus State for people who are using tab to select and then navigate through the websites.

**Для Напарника:** Заметные focus-кольца на кнопках, чипах длительности и полях в /app для клавиатурных и скринридерных пользователей.


### Плейсхолдер не заменяет label: скринридер читает именно label  ·  [08:26](https://youtu.be/-ao_Kc_8rpE?t=506)
**Мысль:** Минималистичные формы без подписей над полем делают поле непонятным для людей со скринридерами.

> All right one more thing is labels so these days a lot of kind of trendy or this kind of design that you might see on dribble or stuff like that when people are designing forms it's very kind of minimalistic right so it's like the example on the left they're not even using a label for the form and by label is what you see on the right when you have kind of tax that is above the form field so a lot of times people would just use what's called the placeholder text inside the label to specify what this label is for so again like the example on the left you can see that the full name is actually not a label it's what's called a placeholder text now the problem with this is as I've mentioned some people use tools that read the websites to them and when there is no label they're not gonna be able to know what this form field is just because the website readers actually read the labels of the form so you have to have kind of a label for the form just build it this way otherwise they won't be able to know what's this form is for and won't be able to fill it up.

**Для Напарника:** Форма имени и чек-ина на Доме и поле шага на Фокусе: настоящий (хотя бы визуально скрытый) label, а не только placeholder.

> So i broke my ankle and i woke up in the hospital i had no idea where i was because it all looks the same and so i asked the nurse i said can you write the hospital number up on the board and thankfully there's a whiteboard it wasn't some crazy like you know windows nt system or something like that and so i said just write it in like you know 200 point text so i can see it at all times even if you know i'm on painkillers and i can't you know really function and so i had my cell phone and i was like hey you can you know you can visit me at the hospital here's my room number and i would just routinely forget things but at least that thing was right there always ambiently telling me what room i was in so when possible always use the least technical solution and all you know only use the digital form if you if you absolutely have to.

# 🎬 Amber Case - ConveyUX 2017 Session Preview — ConveyUX


### Звук по умолчанию тихий, громкость повышает человек  ·  [05:35](https://youtu.be/jXlDWeY9uYw?t=335)
**Мысль:** 30–40% проблем у людей — не с самим устройством, а с тем, как звучит сигнал. В малом пространстве по умолчанию тихо, громче — по выбору.

> I'm working on a book very quietly right now on sound design for products, just the sound design because what I noticed when writing the book on calm technology is that maybe 30% or 40% of people that I talk to had issues not necessarily with the object, but the way the alert sounded when they had the alert. In some cases, you want a really obnoxious alert, you want a kind of annoying alarm clock sound so that it jars you awake. You want a very, very loud fire alarm because you want it to wake somebody up, but if you have a washer or dryer that is in a small apartment or condo like a lot of people are living in today, you don't want that thing to be really noisy. You want to buy a product based on how little it sounds. When it's done, you're probably going to be in the same room with it, what happens if you come home and you want to run your laundry at three in the morning or your dishwasher? I've encountered ... What's happening a lot is people are moving from these larger houses in the suburbs to these smaller places, and why you have really loud sounding washers and dryers and appliances in these larger houses is because they were far away. Often, the washer and dryer was in the basement and it needed to be incredibly loud so you could hear it through the floorboards. If you're in a really small place, the best thing you can do for your customers is allow a variable sound setting or by default, have it be quite low and allow people to turn it up.

**Для Напарника:** Звук костра и сигналы Напарника по умолчанию тихие, с явной регулировкой громкости.



# 🎬 Animal Crossing New Horizons: DEVELOPERS REVEAL HOW ANIMALS WERE CREATED (Game Conference Update) — Mayor Mori


### Узнаваемый силуэт даёт бесконечные вариации персонажей  ·  [01:25](https://youtu.be/6tb1pVmAPso?t=85)
**Мысль:** Дизайн начинают с силуэта, который узнаётся везде, и добавляют черты через личность и увлечения, а не внешние признаки. Всё разнообразие кошек выведено из одного силуэта.

> Now when starting out the design process or the upgrade from their previous looks the developers begin with a silhouette that could be recognized no matter whereabouts they are as you can see this is clearly a cat silhouette so there's no surprise here and sets up the designer with a blank canvas so many cats can be imagined during the design process it was said that it's easier to design a unique cat character by suggesting personality traits and hobbies rather than being limited to actual features hence the basic silhouette now one example given was if fluffy cheeks were an attribute from the start it would be hard to imagine multiple cat characteristics but sticking with the identifiable silhouette removes any limitations and allows for endless designs it's pretty clever thinking now using this technique animal crossing characters can be created with different textures based on a common recognizable appearance the cat silhouette which of course makes it easier to see what species they are whilst also giving enough scope to widen their individuality this screenshot shows a variety of different types of real world cats such as calico himalayan and siamese as well as other familiar appearances such as kabuki makeups and of course an orange for some reason as you can see here they're all clearly cats but all derived from a single silhouette which is really awesome.

**Для Напарника:** Закрепить силуэт кота Напарника как нерушимую основу, а варианты (позы, предметы, настроение) строить поверх него.



# 🎬 Animating a Head Turn : The Important Exercise Everyone Forgets! — BaM Animation


### Простое движение как тест дизайна: выразителен ли он и легко ли анимируется  ·  [00:00](https://youtu.be/3Mik2Gcrq-g?t=0)
**Мысль:** Поворот головы показывает, оживает ли персонаж и не слишком ли дизайн сложен и жёсток; он помогает выбрать стиль движения под пропорции дизайна.

> A head turn is a basic animation movement that should be an early exercise when learning animation in film. It's mainly used as a reaction shot. Because it's a very simple movement, it's often used as a way to test the static design for an actual animation pipeline. When you do this exercise, your character will not only come to life for the first time, but also you'll be able to recognize if the design is expressive and easy to animate or if the design is over complicated and stiff. You will be able to decide what type of movement will look best with your design. A character with realistic proportions may look bizarre if it is moving with a ton of smears or squashes and stretches like a Looney Tunes character.

**Для Напарника:** Прогонять кота через простой цикл (поворот головы, моргание) перед тем как дорабатывать выражения: если дизайн «не оживает», упростить.


### Убери мелкие детали, они не работают в анимации  ·  [02:03](https://youtu.be/3Mik2Gcrq-g?t=123)
**Мысль:** Анимацию приходится перерисовывать 24 раза в секунду, поэтому веснушки, лишние линии и тени хороши для иллюстрации, но мешают в движении.

> Animation is a handmade medium that has to be redrawn 24 times a second, and characters with lots of details are really difficult to animate. And Willow here has a ton of tiny extraneous details that look good for illustration, but would not work well for animation. The freckles, the extra ey lines, and the nose shadows all need to go. And we're going to simplify her hair a bit, too.

**Для Напарника:** Спрайты кота и ориентиров на острове держать без мелкой детализации, чтобы они читались и плавно двигались на мобильном.


### Планирование и тамбнейлы: идея движения, замах для отскока  ·  [02:36](https://youtu.be/3Mik2Gcrq-g?t=156)
**Мысль:** Планирование — основа качественного исполнения: сначала черновые рисунки, затем поза-замах (anticipation), добавляющая подскок, и преувеличенная реакция в нужном кадре.

> They are two things that are at odds with each other. But in animation and other art forms where there can be objective quality, planning is a fundamental component of good execution. So, we need to come up with an idea for the motion we want and how we're going to get there. Like, there are so many options for how she could turn her head. For example, in a very mechanical sense, she could just move her head from A to B without emotion or feeling. She could ease super slowly at first and then snap. Or we could have her sort of jump up and notice something and then kind of snap turn. I kind of like the last one. So, let's try to figure out how to convert this motion into drawings. I'm going to do an initial series of exploratory drawings where I'm just going to try to figure out how it's going to work. This is called thumbnailing, and it's how animators eloos plan the acting. We need to go from here to here. And I want her to jump up a little bit. So, I'll add a drawing in here and then add some space on the timeline before she goes to pose B. This new drawing here is going to be the frame where her eyes are kind of wide and it'll be just a little bit exaggerated. As you can kind of see, Brent is drawing on top of other drawings so that he can keep track of things. This method of drawing over top of barely visible layers is called potato skinning. Well, it's not actually called that, but if you do want to correct me in the comments, go for it so that we can get that engagement up. Okay, let's add two more drawings to this. The first one will be the anticipation pose where she actually moves down first before coming up so that we get sort of a bounce up motion.

**Для Напарника:** Реакция кота на находку: сначала лёгкое приседание, потом подъём; продумать идею движения до CSS-реализации.


### Не ставь промежуточный кадр ровно посередине: динамичные интервалы живее  ·  [04:14](https://youtu.be/3Mik2Gcrq-g?t=254)
**Мысль:** Равномерное расстояние между кадрами выглядит механически; ближе к первому кадру ставь промежуточный, чтобы получить плавный вход и щелчок. Мозг достраивает дугу по одному кадру.

> Breakdown drawings is to put a drawing exactly in the center of the animation. That creates a situation where you have even spacing between your drawings and it looks very mechanical. What we want to do is put a drawing somewhere close to the first frame so that she kind of eases into the turn and snaps. This is what it looks like on a graph. Imagine this ball is the movement of her face. The frames are all spaced together super evenly. Like nobody moves like this. And on this graph, I've got a lot more areas of close together frames and dynamic spacing. This motion is going to look a lot more interesting and breathe life into the character. Also, check this out. Even though there is only one frame at the bottom, we still read the motion as a smooth arc. Your brain fills in the implied movement. Think about this stuff the next time you animate. Like if a character is reaching down to grab something, does it happen in a bunch of super evenly spaced drawings like a robot? Or is there an opportunity to have dynamic motion?

**Для Напарника:** Кривые easing для кота и острова: неравномерные (cubic-bezier с мягким стартом и резким финалом), а не linear.


### Ключевые позы держат движение; вторичная анимация убирает «пластик»  ·  [06:47](https://youtu.be/3Mik2Gcrq-g?t=407)
**Мысль:** Ключевые позы рисуют лучше всего, промежуточные могут выглядеть странно, но служат плавности. Волосы, которые запаздывают и успокаиваются после движения, не дают персонажу выглядеть жёстким пластиком.

> Trace the character model to keep the design consistent and I will spend more time roughing out the key poses which are here to make these drawings the best. The key poses of animation are what really hold the motion together but the other drawings like the breakdowns and the in-betweens are more about facilitating the movement. So often times they may look a bit strange because they were trying to fill in from A to B while maintaining the right spacing. So, people that screenshot these frames in order to make fun of them are just revealing that they don't know how animation works. Wow, look at that. We have our rough pass complete. Also, check out these frames here. The hair drags behind and then settles after the head movement is complete. This is called secondary animation. And it's good to have on parts of your characters that would move independently so that you can avoid having your characters feel like they're made out of rigid plastic.

**Для Напарника:** Уши, хвост и усы кота запаздывают относительно тела при поворотах и реакциях (overlap), а не двигаются как один блок.

> When it comes to image chats, my recommendation is to start a new chat frequently, especially when you notice it's going in the wrong direction because for image-based chats, it's usually pretty hard to correct course.

**Для Напарника:** Процесс генерации иллюстраций для острова/кота: держать эталонную базу и стартовать новый чат при дрейфе стиля.


### Не просить ИИ слишком много за раз: дробить правки  ·  [06:24](https://youtu.be/JDwxt9fHofk?t=384)
**Мысль:** Сразу много изменений ломают исходный стиль картинки; одна правка за запрос сохраняет стиль.

> One mistake I'm seeing a lot of people make when they attempt this is they ask Chachi PT a bit too much in their request. Let me show you guys exactly what I mean by this. It's probably easier with an example. I have this other illustration here and this is another illustration that my fiance made. This is of our dog Luna. So again, this is handdrawn. So, I'm just going to drag it in here and say, can you modify it so that it's a gray cat and the background is actually a coffee shop and instead of a duck, it's a pizza and instead of the iced tea, it is a hot cup of coffee and I want the table to be blue instead of green. If you ask it to do all of these things at once, the problem is it actually can follow these instructions. But what I've noticed is it starts completely just not following the base style of the image you give it. And so that is the biggest problem that I've seen people do. And then when they see this, they obviously get frustrated and they're like, "ChachiPT doesn't work." The secret is to actually chunk this into individual requests and go very slowly so that Chachi PT has a good chance of actually just maintaining the original base style.

**Для Напарника:** Генерация иллюстраций для Напарника: по одной правке на запрос от эталонного кота.



# 🎬 Best Practices for Reducing Extraneous Cognitive Load — J.D. Coleman


### Сигналы экономно + когерентность: иллюстрация, а не декорация  ·  [12:27](https://youtu.be/1fEC5rFCATw?t=747)
**Мысль:** Выделение (жирный, подчёркивание) снижает нагрузку, только если применяется редко; всё, что не служит цели (фоновые звуки, лишние картинки), увеличивает нагрузку.

> One of the easiest ways to reduce cognitive load is by signaling provides cues that highlight the organization of essential material thereby reducing extraneous cognitive load throughout the content examples of signaling include bolding underlining highlighting and organizing content into bullets one thing to note is that signaling should be used sparingly to be most defective and yet another best practice in designing to reduce extraneous cognitive load includes the coherence principle designing with the coherence principle in mind includes excluding extraneous words sounds and video everything that you include in your content should align to the established learning objectives remember illustration not decoration this includes background sound non-essential images and any additional treatment that does not somehow aligned to the learning goals.

**Для Напарника:** Согласуется с правилом CLAUDE.md: не добавлять glow/анимации без цели; один акцент на экран.



# 🎬 Bringing Simplicity to Complexity: How to Design at Scale | Jonah Jones | TEDxGlasgow — TEDx Talks


### Прорыв: искать, чем люди похожи, а не различаются (ориентиры вместо названий улиц)  ·  [05:15](https://youtu.be/cgKMuaW-Yw8?t=315)
**Мысль:** В Индии и Японии люди описывают путь ориентирами, а названия улиц - лишь один из видов ориентиров. Использование всех типов ориентиров решило задачу для всего мира.

> But the breakthrough came when we stopped thinking about all the ways in which people were different and started focusing on the ways in which everyone's the same. So, if you ask for directions in India today, people won't give you an address or a street name or any of this stuff. They'll say things like, "It's by the temple." Or "Go past the river." Or "It's next to the school." They use landmarks to help you get around. And the map reflects this. There may be no street names on this map, but you can see that there's lots of landmarks here that the local people use to get around. Now, in Japan, the situation's actually similar. Because of that really complex address system we talked about, people use landmarks instead of street names to get around. And again, you can see this on the map. No street names, loads and loads of local landmarks here. Now, it turns out that it doesn't matter where you go in the world, from Tokyo to Bangalore to Glasgow, if you ask someone on the street how to get around, they'll describe how to do it using landmarks as well as street names. And suddenly, you realize, if you look outside this building here, everything's a landmark. The restaurant in the bottom right is a landmark. The train station is a landmark. The big buildings are landmarks. The river is a landmark. And the street names, they're just another type of landmark. It turns out that Google Maps had been using landmarks for directions all along. It's just that they'd only been using one specific type of landmark. So, this insight, the Maps team went about upgrading the directions to use all the different types of landmarks that are available. Sometimes using street names when they're relevant, other times using something else. So, now in India, when you ask for directions, Google Maps gives you something like this. Continue past KMK Complex. Turn left at Gode Temple. Your destination is on the left opposite the big banyan tree. That's a great landmark, right? So, by focusing on the ways that people were similar, instead of focusing on the ways in which people were different, the Google Maps team were able to help people get around, no matter where they are in the world.

**Для Напарника:** Ориентиры острова как универсальные якоря памяти и прогресса: люди ориентируются по знакомым местам, а не по цифрам.

> So ingwa kamprad the founder of ikea invented the flat pack in an attempt to make delivery of furniture more cost efficient and effective the flat pack system is based on loose parts bolts screws wooden pieces etc but if they're assembled in the correct way make up a piece of furniture a predefined piece of furniture and the idea is to be able to reuse as many pieces as possible in as many furniture items as possible and to make the assembly as fool proof as possible as well and it has to be clear simple effective so that anyone no matter who you are can assemble a piece of furniture yeah right so this is actually what we're aiming for this is our north star if we can build a system that is as simple and as effective as the ikea flat pack with reusable pieces if correctly put together they will create amazing user experiences and then we know that we will be.

# 🎬 Calm Technology by Amber Case: 11 Minute Summary — SnapTale Audiobook Summaries


### Calm tech 4–8: дополнять человека, не говорить, работать при сбое, минимум  ·  [05:13](https://youtu.be/2ngmtV3_TxI?t=313)
**Мысль:** Технология дополняет, а не заменяет; даёт резерв при отказе; решает проблему минимумом средств; уважает социальные нормы.

> Fourth technology should amplify the best of both technology and Humanity the goal of well-designed technology should be to complement not replace human abilities computers must also understand context to facilitate communication effective technology uses human emotional cues to improve user experience fifth technology can communicate but it shouldn't need to speak technology must communicate with users to achieve its goal but it should engage only peripheral attention voice activated assistants often require a user's full attention making them a problematic area for Cal technology designers should use human senses effectively such as sight sound or touch to minimize intrusion sixth technology should work even when it fails heavily engineered passenger Jets can glide even if their engines fail similarly smaller technology must provide backup options to mitigate disappointment when it fails for example users should be able to silence a smoke detect even if it's difficult to turn off seventh the right amount of technology is the minimum required to solve the problem technology should simplify solutions to improve Effectiveness and minimize complexity this allows technology to disappear into our environment and become unobtrusive finally technology should respect social norms users expect technology to make their lives easier and not disrupt their routines designers should create products that enhance existing social norms rather than displace them.

**Для Напарника:** Когда ИИ недоступен, кот остаётся полезным по заготовкам; не плодить экранов и функций сверх нужного.



# 🎬 CHARACTER DESIGN - Step by Step Tutorial | Drawinglikeasir — Draw like a Sir


### Шаг ноль: личность и «вайб» персонажа до рисования  ·  [00:38](https://youtu.be/bUCx3WW8s3g?t=38)
**Мысль:** Без идеи личности персонаж получается пресным; сначала формулируется эмоция/настроение, потом рисуется.

> So let's start at step number.. zero. Because before drawing your character, you first need some basic idea about him or her. You know, like the age or gender of your character. But most importantly, you need to have an idea about their personality. If you are unexperienced and you started out drawing a character design without any idea of your character's identity, it's gonna end up looking pretty bland. That's actually a big problem with anime. Since it's such a cutthroat industry, character designs and anime original shows tend to look pretty generic because it's quantity over quality at this point. So before you start out, think of a vibe or an emotion your character should portray, like for example, aggressive or arrogant, devious, confident, trauma.

**Для Напарника:** Зафиксировать письменно вайб кота-напарника (тёплый, рядом, не судит) и сверять каждый новый вариант позы и выражения с ним.


### Детали должны нести вайб, а не просто выделять  ·  [03:05](https://youtu.be/bUCx3WW8s3g?t=185)
**Мысль:** Дизайн персонажа начинается с базы, а не с деталей; каждый добавленный элемент должен передавать задуманную эмоцию, иначе получается случайный набор.

> So even if you just keep one thing from this video, please remember that character design isn't about adding details to desperately make your character unique and character design starts with the very base. Yeah. Even if your character is athletic and fit, there are several ways to draw muscles, like bulky, lean and slim. If you stick with one physique for all of your characters, even if they are muscular, this field's very samey. Now that you have your base, we can further work on your character. And don't ever forget, we want to always have the vibe in mind we are going for. Everything you're adding should be something that's actually conveying this vibe or emotion. If you are just adding random stuff for the sake of making your character design look unique, all you are getting at the end is a character that looks like an NFT.

**Для Напарника:** Любой аксессуар или украшение острова и кота добавлять только если он усиливает тепло и присутствие, а не «для красоты».



# 🎬 Config 2024: A modern definition of design craft (Ethan Eissman, SVP Design, Slack) | Figma — Figma


### Полезность, юзабилити и крафт: крафт даёт положительную эмоцию  ·  [01:46](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=106)
**Мысль:** Утилита и юзабилити считаются обязательными, а крафт это то, как продукт ощущается; хорошо сделанный продукт вызывает положительную эмоцию, плохой нейтральную или отрицательную. Все три вместе дают великий дизайн.

> So let's take a big step back and talk about the qualities that go into software design we oftentimes land in three big areas utility usability and craft so utility is about whether the software actually serves a purpose is some to people usability is whether the software can actually like work and whether people can make use of the software and then there's craft is about how the product feels to use in a sign of well-crafted software is a product that elicits a positive emotional response in the person who uses it and that's in opposition to poorly crafted products which often elicit negative or at best neutral responses now what's interesting here is the VIN diagram created when you combine these three product qualities together and we can see that when all three are combined we have the chance to create a product that has great design but when we only have two factors we risk negative experiences.

**Для Напарника:** Обосновывать полировку (хаптика, анимации, голос кота) как отдельное измерение ценности, не как украшение.


### Анимация должна имитировать лучший реальный опыт (дверца с доводчиком)  ·  [04:01](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=241)
**Мысль:** Плавное замедление к концу и щелчок воспринимаются приятнее, чем резкое схлопывание; движение объектов софта должно повторять лучшие физические переживания.

> Animation or the way the software objects in the software move do they snap immediately from point A to B or do they transition smoothly know when you think about animation it's it's about really mimicking an experience that exists in the real world right and ideally it's mimicking exper the best types of real world experience it like when you have a soft closed cabinet dorm you know when you close it kind of goes fast and then at the end it eases to close and then it clicks right at the end that feels amazing feels much better than a door that you just slam shut.

**Для Напарника:** Кривые easing для появления реплик, роста ориентиров и завершения таймера делать с мягким торможением и точкой «щелчка» (в паре с хаптикой на событие).


### Тактильность из индустриального дизайна: будильник Apple Watch  ·  [04:58](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=298)
**Мысль:** Вибро-отклик на тиках колёсика делает установку будильника фиджет-приятной саму по себе, даже без цели; эргономика плюс материальность переносятся в софт.

> Now craft also brings forward the best in industrial design industrial design is about marrying ergonomics the way something feels to be used with materiality or the combinations of materials that go into the product when we think about bringing ideas from industrial design into software the result is an experience that actually feels tactile right it feels more like the real world in tactile one of my favorite examples of this is setting the alarm on the Apple watch who here has tried doing that it's a pretty awesome experience when you use the jog dial you get these like bumpy feedback vibrations as you go over the hands or go over the five minute ticks and it feels fidget you just want to play with it for its own sake even if you don't want to set the alarm.

**Для Напарника:** Хаптика на выборе длительности в пикере (hapticThreshold) уже следует этой логике; держать ощущение 'щелчков' по шагам значения.


### Самый удовлетворяющий чекбокс: крафт на главном действии  ·  [06:16](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=376)
**Мысль:** Приложение привычек вложило графику, 3D, анимацию, звук и интерактивность в единственное главное действие (отметить привычку), чтобы дать дофаминовую награду и закрепить цикл. Крафт служит цели продукта.

> Let's talk about an example of a software company that dives deep into craft now I love this story I'm not sure if anybody you saw Andy's talk here config it's he's incredible he's is just a master of crap so Andy Ellen of not boring software wrote about how they labored over and over again to design and build the world's most satisfying checkbox they did this for their habits app this app is basically meant to help people build habits right so you state what you want to do in the app and then you get to check it off but instead of just a boring checkbox they wanted to create the world's most satisfying checkbox to complete that habit because that was the entire point of the app complete a habit click the check box that was it so here's the point of that most satisfying check box they wanted to feel like a reward because when you get a reward you're releases a little bit of dopamine and once you get that hit of dopamine your mind Associates the action you took with the dopamine hit and that's the Neuroscience of habits they're simp they're simply dopamine reward Loops so if you haven't read Andy's article yet or his articles he's written a number of these they're amazing he describes using all of the dimensions of craft we discussed in building this checkbox they use graphic design 3d design motion animation sound design and of course interactivity all to make that most important checkbox feel just incredible so you get the dopamine hit the reward from clean completing the habit and you do it again and again so it's craft with a purpose and a goal in the product.

**Для Напарника:** Финал сессии Фокус и RevealIsland и есть такой «чекбокс»: все измерения крафта (анимация, звук, хаптика) собирать вокруг момента завершения, а не размазывать по экранам.


### Иерархия качеств дизайна: функциональность, надёжность, юзабилити, удовольствие  ·  [07:44](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=464)
**Мысль:** Наверху пирамиды потребностей стоит «приятно пользоваться», а доступ к ней даёт крафт, но только после надёжной основы.

> Hierarchy of needs right well this is the hierarchy of design qualities at the Baseline the software is functional it works great then it's reliable performant it's free of bugs then it's usable and then finally it's a pleasure to use and the way to think about this Apex T level is by inserting craft into the experience.

**Для Напарника:** Не полировать визуал раньше, чем таймер и сохранение прогресса надёжны.


### Планировать крафт заранее: минимальный любимый продукт вместо минимального жизнеспособного  ·  [15:41](https://youtu.be/tNGDgK0aMjA?t=941)
**Мысль:** Крафт требует времени и итераций, поэтому закладывается в расписание; цель minimal lovable product, а не MVP.

> To make a plan for it from the start craft takes time we bake it into our schedule now this is a bigger conversation but I think there's a real opportunity for us to make sure that we scope projects to attain something that is a minimal level of Love minimal lovable product versus a minimal viable product right that we're hope will Fast follow and make better and another way that we attain craft at slack is through the active prototyping now it is impossible to attain a high level of craft in one try it is the nature of craft that it requires iteration why because craft is all about refinement honing something never settling tweaking it until it's just right always having this desire to make it better.

**Для Напарника:** Делать MVP-экраны Напарника сразу с тёплым тоном и одним продуманным моментом радости.



# 🎬 Core Game Loops — Timothy Cain


### Core loop и дизайн-столпы — разные вещи  ·  [06:38](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=398)
**Мысль:** Столпы объясняют, как каждая фича служит замыслу; core loop — те из фич, которые игроки используют чаще всего. Core loop должен касаться всех столпов, остальные фичи могут их не затрагивать.

> Let me make a big distinguish here between core game loops and design pillars I have a whole video on design pillars and I'll link it below they are not the same thing the design pillars are there to explain how every feature that you've selected to be in the game serves those pillars but a core game Loop is you saying okay of all those features that we've put in the game that serve the design pillars these are the ones I expect most of the players to experience most of the time and there's difference there because you may have things that you put in like let's say you put in reactivity that's something I love putting in my game my games reactivity might be a design pillar which means when you put in features your you're going to always ask the question of how does the player what the player does when they interact with this feature come back to them how are they being told hey here's how good you're doing with this thing the core game Loop would be those features that the player does a lot you would expect all of them to have large amounts of reactivity because if this is what the player is doing most of the time it better be supporting that design pillar of reactivity because I've talked about you can occasionally have a feature that doesn't support all the design pillars like you may put something in that's if humor is one of your design pillars you may have something in the game that's not that funny as long as overall the game is funny but for your core game Loop it should probably touch on all your pillars because it's these features are what your the player is doing a lot it should it should re it should have a basis in those design pillars.

**Для Напарника:** Ввести столпы Напарника (тепло, без стыда, дофамин) и проверять, что core loop затрагивает каждый из них.


### Core loop задаёт приоритеты, расписание и оптимизацию  ·  [08:32](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=512)
**Мысль:** Он подсказывает, что делать в первую очередь, что можно выбросить при нехватке времени и где искать баги: частые пути важнее редких.

> Your core game Loop tells your entire team what to focus on because here are the features of this big design document here are the features that are the most important to get correct to get in early to get them correct it will help you it will help the production staff decide what to prioritize because when there are features that have to be done you're like well these are part of the core game Loop they have to be done you could have a fun it's like side quests if you have to drop a side quest because you run out of time that's okay because it's not part of the main story your features help you schedule in the same way this is a great feature it's fun but is it part of the core game Loop and if it's not it can go you may not want it to go but it can go similarly when you get to the end of the game this tells you where to spend a lot of your time optimizing because if core game Loop features like I said it's where most players spend most of their time you want that to be optimized you want a smooth UI experience you want a good frame rate you want to make sure that there are no bugs in that core game Loop because that's what most of your players are going to spend most of their time doing if there's a you know an occasional bug that occurs from some obscure thing that the player can maybe do like maybe if they craft one item at a particular table and give it to a particular NPC and it causes that NPC to go in a bad State you want to fix that but if you had to prioritize that bug against oh yeah if you ever shoot a gun at an NPC that has more than five hit points sometimes the game crashes fix that.

**Для Напарника:** Приоритизировать полировку и баги Дома и Фокуса (основные пути), а не редких экранов.


### Чем больше core loop, тем больше расписание и команда  ·  [11:52](https://youtu.be/aDE69s3pp7M?t=712)
**Мысль:** Большая вариативность режимов (боевой, диалог, стелс) множит работу; рекомендация держать core loop максимально сфокусированным.

> One last thing about core game Loop and I'm saying this from experience the bigger your core game Loop the longer it takes you to write it down the more features that are part of it the harder that game Loop will be to implement I say this from experience because when I make games that I say I want people to be able to play them all these different ways I want them to talk through it and fight through it and stealth through it and go through primarily with companion support that just added a lot of work and time to your schedule because if that's the core game Loop that you expect most of the players to be doing it's got to be good and of course like I've always said that then translates a big core game Loop will translate into a big schedule which will translate into a big team and a lot of money so it's in your best interest to try to get your core game loop as laser focused as possible but understand that some games will have big core game Loops it's just always in your best interest it's in your best interest it's in your team's best interest for knowing what they should work on and it's in the Publishers best interest for knowing what game they're buying and how you're going to respond to suggestions to change it's in everybody's best interest to get that core game Loop defined as early and as specifically as you can.

**Для Напарника:** Держать core loop Напарника узким: один основной путь вместо множества режимов.



# 🎬 Cozy Games Need an Edge — auranym


### Три критерия уютной игры  ·  [03:21](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=201)
**Мысль:** Простые и понятные механики, медленный методичный геймплей, намеренно заложенное ощущение комфорта.

> Feel free to disagree with these, but to me, a cozy game needs to fit three criteria. One, it has mechanics that are simple and easy to grasp. Two, it has slow, methodical gameplay. Three, the feeling of comfort is common and intended by the developers.

**Для Напарника:** Рабочая проверка для любой новой фичи Напарника по трём критериям: просто, медленно, намеренно тепло.


### Несовершенство арта делает мир человеческим, но не продаёт сам  ·  [04:50](https://youtu.be/x-3F1TRYq00?t=290)
**Мысль:** Неровная пиксельная графика и «кривые» пропорции показывают живых авторов, поэтому игре верят. Но этот приём стал распространённым, и одного арт-направления не хватает для глубины.

> This aspect of authenticity is essential for a good cozy game to me. The world of the game needs to feel like it was made by real people with their own thoughts and feelings. And if we think of a game as a piece of art meant to express those thoughts and feelings, then the art style itself is a very raw and direct way to engage with the people who crafted it. When you look at Stardew Valley and its highly textured pixel art and wonky building proportions, you can tell that it was made by someone. Someone whose art style uses a lot of dithering and emphasizes shadows and bright colors. Someone who doesn't care that pixels don't all conform to the same pixel grid and doesn't care to limit their color palette size. Someone who was possibly learning it all on the fly and who just wanted to make a game for you and I to enjoy. I think this comes through in A Short Hike as well with its low-poly models at a downscaled resolution, and all the way back in the original Animal Crossing with its highly stylized, crunchy textures. These games were made by real people and this can be discerned, I think, based on the art style alone. The art is imperfect but through those imperfections, those rough edges, the human being behind these games can be seen and felt. The brush strokes, the pixels placed, the models crafted, all done by human hands. I think this sort of human imperfection element has been noticed by game developers, and it's now being utilized as an intentional design choice. In A Little to the Left, there's a hand-drawn vibe conveyed through the rough edges on each of the object drawings. In Potion Craft, it mimics the style of medieval drawings, which are sort of known to be wonky and weird by today's standards. While I do appreciate that this element of human imperfection can help a cozy game be successful, I also think that because it's now a common technique, it means that it alone cannot sell a cozy game. By that, I mean good art direction doesn't necessarily allow a game to feel fulfilling.

**Для Напарника:** Допустить в иллюстрациях кота и острова лёгкую рукотворность, но не рассчитывать на неё как на единственное отличие.



# 🎬 Creating Our Duolingo Characters • Greg Hartman • Duocon 2020 — Duolingo


### Имя персонажа: критерии личности, произносимости и написания  ·  [06:37](https://youtu.be/m-3-D7S0piw?t=397)
**Мысль:** Имя должно отражать характер, легко произноситься на многих языках и писаться одинаково; примеры отклонённых имён (Jun, Pete).

> We figured this would only take like a week or two, until we realized naming is really hard, especially when spanning many languages. We knew we wanted everyone, no matter where in the world they were using Duolingo to refer to our characters by the same name. This proved more difficult than we originally thought. Finding a name that we, one, thought reflected the character's personality. Two, was a name that could be easily pronounced in many languages. And three was spelled similarly, if not identically across all courses. See, this last one is especially important because there are... so there wasn't any confusion around seeing a character's name in your native language versus the language you were learning, which could often appear on the same screen. An example of a name I really liked for this character, but did not fit the criteria was Jun. Jun would have been pronounced as Hoon in Spanish so that name was kind of out. Another example that I liked was Pete, but Pete, if not pronounced exactly right in French means fart. So we had to scrap that name too. So you take those examples and you multiply that by the nine characters we designed then multiply that by the number of languages we accounted for, and your spreadsheet quickly turns from this to...

**Для Напарника:** Имя кота выбирать по критерию простоты произношения и тёплого звучания в русском; проверить на нежелательные созвучия.



# 🎬 Dark Mode Website Design | 6 Tips For Better Dark UI Design — WPTuts


### Тени в тёмной теме: чёрные с настроенной прозрачностью, серые дают свечение  ·  [02:33](https://youtu.be/JNhG2T6dGOY?t=153)
**Мысль:** Серая тень на тёмном фоне выглядит как свечение; близкая к чёрной с прозрачностью около 25% читается как настоящая тень и добавляет объёмность.

> Chances are if you're working in lighter designs you're used to working with either the shade of the background color itself or using gray and that's not going to cut it when we're working with dark mode let me demonstrate if we select the actual background itself we'll go to our effects and we'll set a shadow inside there and we'll set this to be a mid-gray like we're pretty much used to doing and we'll adjust the parameters for this as well to give a more natural looking shadow you can see straight away that looks kind of strange we now get this sort of glow effect around it as opposed to a shadow effect so we need to go ahead and adjust that so let's go back in let's select this one more time choose our background come into our drop shadow and let's set this to be totally black or pretty close to it we're gonna go right the way down we're dealing with 25 opacity in this example but you could adjust that to taste to get a stronger or lighter effect whatever you think works but you'll find as soon as you set that to black now we get a much more natural looking shadow the whole design starts to look a little bit more 3d and we just get a more realistic looking layout so when you're working with dark mode and you're working with shadows go for black or close to black and adjust the opacity to taste to give you a more realistic looking shadow in your designs.

**Для Напарника:** Для карточек над AppBackdrop использовать почти чёрные тени низкой непрозрачности, а свечения оставлять только для событийных наград.



# 🎬 Design Systems at Scale // Episode 1: The Heartache of Design at Scale — Dan Mall

**Для Напарника:** Принцип разработки Напарника: наращивать остров и механики итерациями, начиная с минимального рабочего ядра.



# 🎬 Design Systems at Scale // Episode 4: New Roles & Processes for Design Systems — Dan Mall


### Сделай правильное лёгким: ограничь палитру, убери лишнее  ·  [03:08](https://youtu.be/XVJKR8Jw254?t=188)
**Мысль:** Если красный нельзя использовать, его просто не кладут в систему: неправильное делается трудным. Палитра должна быть не слишком большой и не слишком маленькой.

> And I think a good design system has those kinds of rules that ultimately should come from designers who are going all right let me let me think about what the best experience is going to look like and let me like you said let me make the right thing to do the easiest thing to do by making it hard for people to do the wrong thing if they shouldn't be using red let's just not even have red in the design system at all right well let's limit that palette and so I think one of the things for a designer to be thinking about is what's that small palette that everybody else can use what are the components what are the colors what are the type faces how do we create a system that scales that's not too big and not too small but just the right size for everyone to be able to do their.

# 🎬 Design Systems at Scale // Episode 5: Proven Strategies for Scaling Your Design System — Dan Mall

> Implementation agnostic design properties that might sound a little esoteric but that's what they are so any developer will know what a variable is right you say you know color brand red right so if you're a Target you have color brand red is Target red right or you might have a specific border radius value that you use for all of your digital products or you might have a specific font face you use for all of your products all of these design properties are what help build a brand these you could sort of refer to them as subatomic properties right these are the properties that ultimately make up your user interface.
