# 🎬 How to Make Your Vibecoded Mobile App Feel Human | Product Delight — Build Native Mobile apps with AI


### Иерархия потребностей: приятность на вершине и дешёвые нижние слои  ·  [01:28](https://youtu.be/Hvnk76SKmtc?t=88)
**Мысль:** Функциональность, надёжность и юзабилити теперь дёшевы, поэтому отличать продукт будет четвёртый слой — приятность. Раньше его считали вишенкой на торте.

> I want to share with you a framework that actually is almost 20 years old. So nothing very new about it. It's called the hierarchy of users need. Maybe you've heard about it. It's developed and designed by Aaron Walter. Aaron Walter used to be the head of design at the Mailchimp. And he actually came with this framework saying that for every product to hit in a good way market and the user, it has to go through four layers. So, the first layer is functionality, meaning that the product has to function. It has to work, right? I mean, that's the basic. it has to be reliable, meaning that it has to work all the time for all conditions for all condition and infrastructure. And then, the third layer is about usability. The product has to be easy to use. And that's extremely important, and it's really important to design things in a way that they are easy to use. But what's even more important from this framework is that Aaron said that the top of pyramid is pleasurability. So, the product has to be pleasurable to be used. And a lot of people has been wondering, what does it actually mean to build something pleasurable? And also, the other problem is that it sounded like this comes like the luxury part. It's that cherry on the top. It's the icing on the cake. Who actually cares? And more importantly, that for couple of years ago, building the first three layers has been extremely expensive. Like you need to hire an entire dev team. You need to raise funds. You need to get an office. And so, getting to the fourth layer sounded like a dream, and it was like abandoned for many years. The problem that we're seeing, which is by the way a positive problem that we're living, is that the access to the first three layers is becoming cheaper. It's becoming easier. We can build product in like hackathon days and hours. And so, what will distinguish, what will differentiate your mobile app or your product, is going to be that pleasurability.

**Для Напарника:** Эмоциональная отделка Напарника (кот, остров, тон) — не украшение, а главное конкурентное преимущество.



# 🎬 How To Think Like A Game Designer — Game Maker's Toolkit


### Не копируй механику, не поняв, почему она работает  ·  [00:00](https://youtu.be/iIOIT3dCy5w?t=0)
**Мысль:** Автосейв по чекпоинтам работал в Call of Duty, но убил страх в Alien: Isolation: игроки без опаски бродили, зная, что потеряют пару минут.

> Okay, so during the development of Alien: Isolation, the game had a pretty basic save system. It would automatically cache your data whenever you tripped invisible checkpoints, which were dotted throughout each level. It was added because it was easy to implement, simple to understand - and, in truth, because that's how saving worked in pretty much every game. Design director Gary Napper said "like any development team, we all play a lot of games and each have our favourites. Often our decisions and choices are coloured by the games we play". But... as development went along, the team started to question if this save system was actually the right choice. You see, because players knew that if they died they'd only lose a couple minutes of progress, they would just waltz around the space station without a care in the world. Not ideal... in a horror game. *Alien scream* So while these checkpoints might have worked in Call of Duty and Bioshock - Napper says it was "not the right approach when making a game that is designed from its core to terrify and put people on edge". Because here's the thing. It's totally fine to take influence from other games. Many designers borrow mechanics from their favourite titles - often evolving, combining, and remixing them into something new. But: it’s essential to understand why those mechanics work in one game, before copying and pasting them into yours.

**Для Напарника:** Перед заимствованием механики (стрик, сгорающий прогресс) проверять, какое поведение и чувство она даст в контексте СДВГ-продукта.



# 🎬 How to Work with Non-Designers | Proven UX Design Process — Stuart Studios


### Четыре категории риска (Каган): usability, feasibility, business, value  ·  [03:28](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=208)
**Мысль:** Дизайнер обычно зациклен на usability-риске, а команда думает о четырёх рисках сразу, и они пересекаются; общий язык рисков выравнивает команду.

> Number one is rally around the same risks so when you're designing a building product every decision that you make is going to carry some amount of risk and in marty kagan's book inspired he explicitly calls out four major risk categories and i've been using his framework for years now and anecdotally it has been so helpful for my day-to-day work not only actual designing piece of it but in influencing and communicating and collaborating all right so take a look here are the four risk categories i'm not going to go into real detail here i highly recommend that you check out the book inspired by marty kagan he does a great job of explaining these and going into detail and breaking everything down but for the purpose of this video and just also the way i communicate this in meetings and in presentations i'm going to do it that way just to make it easy so let's go bottom up usability risk this is about if the user can actually figure out what you designed or built pretty straightforward right this is kind of in the wheelhouse of designers now feasibility risk is more about capabilities can the builders engineers actually make it code it build it business risk can the business work with it i know that's a little bit vague but typically it deals with like legal financial you know maybe there's some regulatory or compliance thing going on and the most important probably is going to be value risk so even if everything else checks out and you design it and build it and put it out into the world will the users actually like really buy it or will they actually use it if you make it for them and it's helpful to know that these are all related it's they're not mutually exclusive so there's going to be overlaps also if you change one or you adjust anything it may impact some other risk category.

**Для Напарника:** Для решений по Напарнику взвешивать не только удобство, но и ценностный риск: будет ли человек реально возвращаться к острову и коту.


### Мелкие UX-компромиссы копятся; покажи будущее состояние  ·  [07:28](https://youtu.be/B9ZdpeoPZ7A?t=448)
**Мысль:** Мелкие решения (не тот цвет, кнопка чуть в стороне) складываются в долг; в контексте долгосрочного видения их эффект виден, а решения сегодня готовят завтрашний продукт.

> Align on a future vision and designers i think this one may sting a little bit but hear me out no one intentionally sets out to make a product of terrible ux it's it happens because there's real pressure to meet business outcomes and in those situations usability things can typically get overlooked or it's very easy to overlook them especially with the iterative nature of design and building these days it's even for designers i think it's hard to see what the big deal is of using an off-brand color or just rearranging things to squeeze stuff in or just placing a button slightly somewhere else just to get the job done but you and i know that you know those things compound over time and the effects of it the negative effects of it'll show in the medium to long term but the here and now it may not make a major difference and it may seem completely fine look so it's easier to see the impact of the smaller ux decisions if you place it in context of a more holistic and longer term vision and you can use your design skills to kind of draw that up and help people see what you're talking about because typically non-designer roles they probably have a hard time imagining what you're seeing as designers it may be easier for us and more natural to be able to piece things together and imagine what things could be but that's not true for a lot of other roles so this is where you can really be proactive and help drive alignment and strategy for the product and project that you're working on the other thing i'll mention is that then the usability risks become way more apparent when you're able to see the small little decisions and the impact of it in a future state so oftentimes do showing people what a decision may become is extremely powerful this is also going to be helpful for you as a designer because this is sort of playing chess with the future state of the product or feature that you may be working on so what i mean by this is the decision that you would make here and now just to solve the problem right now will likely be very different from a decision you would make to set yourself up to have the product become something else in the future so by doing this exercise and having that holistic vision the north star of where you're trying to go it's way easier to figure out what types of decisions you need to make sure that you have so that you set yourself up well.

**Для Напарника:** Держать north star Напарника (остров растёт, кот рядом) и сверять мелкие правки с ним, как уже делают жёсткие константы визуального языка.

> Important thing here is to decide what is the primary action what is the common use case of this screen the most common use case is obviously to book a ticket when you're traveling there are two kinds of travel there's a one-way trip and there's a round trip and I have not used this mobile experience before so I'm still pretty confused as to how I can book just a round trip versus just a one-way trip secondly because there are so many items here so much Tech so many images my eyes don't really know where to look so I think the first principle here is to understand what is the core objective of the screen and then understand how can we give maximum real estate to that one primary action without adding elements that can distract you now in design there's a very basic principle that if you can get rid of something cut it away anything that comes on the screen should be absolutely necessary if you can cut away any word any single line of text that is recommended.

A relationship app could be flowers or a love heart. You get the picture. And then you really need to think about how the mascot interacts or feels part of the app and not just plonked in there. You should also consider your own skill level at this stage, too. Don't go animating a 3D Pixar inspired mascot on your first attempt.

**Для Напарника:** Кот у костра как метафора присутствия; держать стиль простым и анимируемым.


### Шаблон маскота + собственный набросок позы + ИИ только полирует  ·  [05:03](https://youtu.be/zmfzCi_fpJc?t=303)
**Мысль:** ИИ хорош в полировке своих идей и посредственных шаблонных идеях; эскиз позы и шаблон сохраняют пропорции.

> This acts as my template and I can ask Chat TPT to manipulate my mascot into any pose I want while keeping the design and proportions consistent. First, I do a rough sketch of a pose and then I use the prompt I want this character doing XYZ in this pose. I provide chat GPT with a mascot template and my sketch. A so cute. It's important to do some of your own creative thinking here. You can't really outsource the design elements to chat GPT. It's really only good at two things. Polishing the ideas you've already come up with or coming up with its own bland, boring ideas. If you sketch your idea first, helps to prompt Chat TPT to provide a better mascot and pose. Plus, it saves you a bit of back and forth trying to get the mascot absolutely perfect.

**Для Напарника:** При создании поз/выражений кота давать ИИ эталонный шаблон и грубый эскиз; идеи придумывает человек.


### Мелкие детали оживляют статичную картинку: хвост, голова, гравитация  ·  [05:47](https://youtu.be/zmfzCi_fpJc?t=347)
**Мысль:** Раздельные слои с независимой анимацией (хвост, поворот головы, лёгкий отскок при посадке) превращают рисунок в живой образ.

> Next, we're going to add this mascot to our app. And I guess you could just add the image into your app and call it a day, but it kind of feels so lifeless and a little bit boring. With a few Swift UI view animations and a few timers, you can turn a static image like this into this. The secret to making it look like a real life mascot comes down to the small details. a wagging tail, a dynamic expression, a rotating head, and gravity. Yes, gravity. When the cat drops into its default position, the head moves slightly and independently of the whole body, and everything kind of bounces a bit slightly when it hits the ground. This is achieved using Swift UI with animation bouncy and a few view layers as well. Then each layer has a different part of the mascot overlaid and independently animated. Seriously, this is worth your time giving your mascot those tiny, subtle qualities. It turns a beautiful design into a living design.

**Для Напарника:** Слои кота анимировать независимо с bouncy-посадкой при появлении; не трогать MascotSvg ради отдельного экрана, только осмысленно в общем компоненте.

# 🎬 Instructional Design Basics: Cognitive Load — Instructional Design Tips


### Эстетика снижает нагрузку до переломной точки, потом увеличивает  ·  [03:52](https://youtu.be/1jvnKTtmqF0?t=232)
**Мысль:** Красота помогает, пока не превращается в «эффектно и грандиозно» с кучей элементов на экране; каждый элемент должен помогать человеку, иначе «ты делаешь обратное».

> This is why when people talk about Aesthetics and learning design it's not just I want to make it look pretty because that's what I like to do or because I want to show off Aesthetics matter in that they can help reduce extraneous cognitive load now there is a limit to that and a Tipping Point where we can very easily Focus too much on making things look impressive and Grand and actually go from reducing to increasing extraneous load by putting so much stuff on the screen it starts to get confusing remember every decision we make in terms of modality in terms of navigation in terms of how content is delivered whether it's digital or human or some combination of the two is all about helping the learner succeed if that is not the reason you're adding something you need to really stop and question yourself hard why is this thing being added if it's just for you if it's to make yourself look clever or to show off or to make it pretty simply for the sake of that please reconsider you are doing the exact opposite of what you think you are doing you are hindering the learner by increasing extraneous loads.

**Для Напарника:** Прямое подтверждение правила CLAUDE.md §10: анимации и глоу только с привязкой к принципу.



# 🎬 Juice it or lose it - a talk by Martin Jonasson & Petri Purho — grapefrukt


### Сочность: максимум отклика на минимум ввода  ·  [00:35](https://youtu.be/Fy0aCDmgnxg?t=35)
**Мысль:** «Сочная» игра отвечает на каждое действие каскадом реакций; сок бывает визуальный, звуковой или в неожиданных текстовых ответах. Авторы утверждают, что он улучшает игру всегда.

> In videogames, juicy things are things that wobble, squirt, bounce around or make cute noises. Sort of a catch-all phrase for things that make the game feel good to interact with. The term juicy comes from this classic Gamasutra article called 'How to prototype a game in under 7 days'. And they say this: a juicy game feels alive and responds to everything you do, tons of cascading action and response for minimal user input, and juice is typically visual or auditory, but it doesn't really need to be: Emily Short mentions on her blog that interactive fiction, which is a text-based game, they can't really do wiggles and squirts much, but they have their own kind of juice: the fun and, sort of unexpected responses you get when you try something in the game that you're not supposed to do. And that's pretty much what juice is about, it's about maximum output for minimum input. - So why juiciness? I recently had the "pleasure" of giving feedback on some student games, and what I noticed was that almost all of them lacked juiciness. That made me realize how important juiciness actually is to a game. Because game design is quite hard and it's impossible to give broad statements that are true for everything. But adding juiciness to your game makes your game better 100% of the time, guaranteed.

**Для Напарника:** Каждый тап в Напарнике (старт сессии, отметка шага) должен вызывать тёплый многослойный отклик кота и острова, при этом без шума ради шума.


### Простейший ease-out: двигайся на 10% к цели каждый кадр  ·  [03:59](https://youtu.be/Fy0aCDmgnxg?t=239)
**Мысль:** Приём, когда значение каждый кадр приближается на долю расстояния: сначала быстро, потом замедляется. Очень просто реализуется и выглядит плавно.

> Basically we're moving x (whatever value that is) 10% of the way we need to go. So at first, it will go fast and then as it approaches its target it will slow down. Looks very nice, very easy to implement, like this, have it run every frame: you're good.

**Для Напарника:** Использовать такой приём для плавных движений кота, счётчиков и появления элементов острова.


### Easing с перелётом, масштаб, вращение и случайная задержка  ·  [04:33](https://youtu.be/Fy0aCDmgnxg?t=273)

It's slowing down slightly. We can do better, though. We can go slightly past our target using a different equation. Look at that! Very luxurious! And there's also crazy equations. You're laughing. This is serious stuff. And the bouncing equation, this is a crazy one: look at it! Amazing. And we can also tween other properties, not just position. We can tween rotation, we can tween scaling. Let's try that. See what happens. Very nice. You see, it's getting somewhere. And the best thing we can do is add a little bit of delay, a random delay to everything. Looks like this. Look at that! > So, unlike easing and tweening, squeezing and stretching isn't an actual programming term that you can Google, but this is this a term we came up with for a bunch of different effects that you can add your game to make things way better. Some of these are done with tweening and easing, or most of these are done with tweening and easing. So we're gonna start out with making the paddle respond a bit. So, instead of... This isn't actually even using any kind of easing, it's using the offset of the mouse pointer and where the paddle is to figure out the scale of x and y. It's a very simple effect to implement. The next thing we're gonna do is we're going to add some extra scaling to the ball. So when the ball hits something, it's gonna scale up to bigger, and it's gonna go smaller with an easing equation and that looks kind of nice and adds a bit of feel to the ball hitting things. Then we're gonna make the ball rotate. So it's gonna rotate in the direction that it's going. We're gonna animate this rotation for a very small subtle effect that's almost impossible to see. We're gonna make also the ball stretch a bit. It's stretching based on its velocity. This looks way better if you have gravity, but it's okay with this. It also looks better if it's actually a ball shape. We're gonna animate the stretching down, so whenever the ball hits something, it's gonna wobble for a bit. And for the last thing, we're gonna add color to the ball, So the ball will get white every time it hits something, and then it's gonna scale back to its normal. So it already feels way better to move, bounce the ball around. But this isn't juicy enough, so we're gonna add some tweening to all of the blocks when something is hit. So when the ball hits something, there's a small scaling effect on all the blocks, which is done with tweening. It's a very simple effect to add. And the last thing we're gonna add: bouncy line. So whenever the ball hits the line, the lines are gonna bounce a bit. Making things feel so much nicer to play around with.

**Для Напарника:** Мягкая реакция кота и предметов острова на события (сквиш при тапе, короткая вспышка) вместо статичных картинок; оставлять медленной и диегетичной.

# 🎬 Level Head - How to Make Your Game's Combat Feel Good — Sunder


### Game feel делает средние игры веселее, чем они есть  ·  [01:10](https://youtu.be/x9e4fUqKmjY?t=70)
**Мысль:** Обратная связь, полировка и атмосфера создают впечатление веселья даже у неглубоких систем; Diablo II не особенно интересна, но отклик на действия приятен.

> But the meat and potatoes of good combat lies in what most people would refer to as game feel. It's an ambiguous title for the feedback given to the player while playing the game, and often referred to when talking about games that have above and beyond polish, atmosphere, and visceral feeling when playing them. It can make mediocre games give the impression of being more fun than they actually are, which is a sneaky tactic that has led some of the highest selling and highest playerbased games out there to lead the market, even if they aren't the most balanced, most polished, or indeed the most original. Diablo II comes to mind in this sense, where the gameplay itself, when examined under a microscope, isn't incredibly deep or super engaging. Much of the fun comes from combining a few moves together and maybe a few legendary items to augment those moves into a set of attacks that makes this happen. Now, what was shown wasn't necessarily a difficult task, but damn, it felt good. Clustering dozens of enemies together and watching them ragdoll across the screen from my ability, that feels powerful. That's me kicking ass and taking names, coring a bunch of demons into one spot and obliterating them into a rain of corpses that litter the air around me until I dash onto the next group to redo the whole process. I actually love Diablo II. I think it's a lot of fun, but I don't think it's a really interesting game. I just think it has some great feedback when I'm fighting enemies.

**Для Напарника:** Качество отклика (звук, анимация, хаптика) в Напарнике может быть главным источником удовольствия, даже при простой механике.


### Игрок должен понимать параметры действия по звуку и виду; бас спас оружие  ·  [02:35](https://youtu.be/x9e4fUqKmjY?t=155)
**Мысль:** Тяжёлые басовые звуки и масса объекта сообщают силу удара (ракетница Halo). Разработчик Bungie лишь усилил бас звука оружия, и инвесторы решили, что оно исправлено.

> First things first, the player needs to know the parameters of their attack. This means that when they punch, slash, or shoot, they get the proper audio and visuals that clue them into exactly how that move works. In order to draw out the most satisfying response, it's important for players to feel like they understand their abilities, so that when they make an attempt to execute those abilities, the outcome is something that they had planned for, expected, and will be pleased with. There are exemptions to this. Of course, it's always fun to subvert player expectation and give them wild things to deal with on the fly. But for core combat, for the main gameplay loop, the player should be able to quickly discern which of their options is the quickest, which is the highest raw damage, which is situationally useful, which is helpful from a distance, etc. So, plan animations and hit effects and sound effects accordingly. When a move deals massive damage, heavy bassy sound effects and flashy visuals can convey this very easily. Think about the rocket launcher from the Halo series. The weapon itself takes up a massive portion of the screen. It's huge and boxy with oversized barrels on the front. It has a tiny pool of ammo. It appears rarely and when you shoot it, it does this. Listen to that bassy launch and that explosion. Watch how the travel time of the rocket is fairly slow and deliberate. Without even having an enemy to test this on, it's fairly clear that this is a high damage weapon and a force to be reckoned with. Put it next to an assault rifle, a pistol, and a plasma rifle, and this is clearly a weapon that feels like it has weight to it. This is a great example of giving the player knowledge of what to expect from their actions taken from this. And it delivers on those expectations, sending enemies reeling and vehicles flying through the air. While the idea of this may seem minor, I'll be talking about it a lot. So, allow me to refer to a talk from the Amsterdam Control Conference in 2013 that you may be familiar with on this subject. It's by Yan Villim Neyon, one half of Dutch independent game studio Lombard, creators of Ridiculous Fishing, Nuclear Throne, Lofrowousers, etc. At one point in the talk, he recalls speaking with the developer from Boware, who was in a meeting with investors for the game that they were working on. To paraphrase the situation, essentially the investors told them to fix a certain gun in the game being worked on, saying it wasn't balanced and it was a bad weapon. Said developer had an idea, and instead of fixing numbers or how the weapon worked, all they did was open up the sound files for the gun and apply some liberal bass boosting to make it sound more powerful. The next time he showed the build to the same people that had said it was a bad weapon, they reported back saying it was fixed and it felt great.

**Для Напарника:** Звуки в Напарнике должны выражать «вес» события: тяжёлое, глубокое для завершения сессии, лёгкое для мелкого шага.


### Покажи эффективность действия: враг реагирует на удар  ·  [04:47](https://youtu.be/x9e4fUqKmjY?t=287)
**Мысль:** Вспышки, отбрасывание и звуки боли подтверждают, что действие работает. В Oblivion отсутствие физической реакции врагов ощущается плохо.

> Next up, if you want your players to enjoy hitting things and shooting things, let them know that hitting and shooting things is effective. The easiest example of this comes from back in the low res era of gaming where enemies would flash white or red or yellow when taking a hit. This is positive feedback. It conveys that the player's actions are dealing damage and ultimately moving them towards their goal. But let's take it a step further. It doesn't have to be a flash of light that shows damage, even though that's an effective solution. Enemies can be knocked back or staggered by a heavy hit. Sound clips can be used to show they're feeling the pain of the player's weapon or that their armor isn't holding up well to being assaulted. A great example of this done poorly is in The Elder Scrolls Oblivion. While the sound effects are good enough for the most part, they're basically the only indication of a connecting attack with an enemy aside from directly watching their health bar lower, which in my opinion is not a good way to handle it. The weapons feel like they have weight on the input side, but the output shows no physical effects on the enemies. It's incredibly common to watch a bandit shift from looking in near perfect health to being dead in the span of a single attack. They don't react to the player's input in battle. And for a lack of a better way to say it, that just feels bad,.

**Для Напарника:** Любое нажатие в Напарнике должно визуально подтверждаться реакцией мира, иначе действие кажется не сработавшим.


### Разная сила — разный звук, частицы и реакция  ·  [05:51](https://youtu.be/x9e4fUqKmjY?t=351)
**Мысль:** Автомат и дробовик должны звучать и выглядеть по-разному, показывая силу: игроку не нужно быть всемогущим, нужно соответствие услышанного и увиденного происходящему.

> It's important to tailor all the details to show the player exactly how effective those abilities are. From a baseline standpoint, let's just say you have a first-person shooter with two different starting weapons, an automatic rifle and a shotgun. Firstly, these guns should not have the same firing and hit sound effects, and not even from a standpoint of making them realistic or anything. They should sound distinctly different when used to imply strengths. When unloading the rifle into a shambling zombie, it could maybe slightly slow the zombie down and have sound effects mainly in the mid-range of the spectrum. Small hit particles can show it's doing moderate damage and then plainly visible from the zombie continuing to move forward and attack. The player would be able to see that this weapon will deliver a kill after a short time. All of it tells the player that the rifle is useful but not incredibly powerful. Then when they swap to the shotgun, crank things up. Again, heavy bassy sound when the bullet spread impacts. Much larger splatter and flash particles to designate heavy damage. Instead of a slowdown on the zombies walk, it can straight up knock them back, stagger them, or hey, if it's a point blank shot, just downright deal the killing blow. Just by letting the player use these two weapons first hand, it's clear which does what. It's apparent from the moment the bullet connects that not only is damage being dealt, like in the last step we talked about, but also how much damage. The player doesn't have to feel all powerful for the weapons to feel good. They just need to feel like what they're seeing and hearing and experiencing matches what's actually being done by their attacks,.

**Для Напарника:** Различать малые, средние и большие события (шаг, сессия, ориентир) звуком, анимацией и хаптикой пропорционально.



# 🎬 Make Any Game Feel AMAZING: 7 Juice Tricks from "Juice It or Lose It" (JavaScript Tutorial) — Back To Code


### Juice - слой обратной связи: подтверждает каждое действие  ·  [01:19](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=79)
**Мысль:** Juice - это не новые механики, уровни или арт, а отклик на ввод; игра подтверждает каждое действие, и игрок чувствует себя услышанным.

> So, what exactly is Juice? Steve Swing's book, Game Field, defines game feel as realtime control of virtual objects in a simulated space with interactions emphasized by polish. Juice is that polish. It's the feedback layer. Everything the game does to answer the player. When you press a button, something should react clearly and generously. Notice what juice is not. It's not new mechanics, not new levels, and not better art. It's the response. A juicy game confirms every action so the player always feels heard.

**Для Напарника:** Каждое касание в Напарнике получает ясный, щедрый, но тихий отклик.


### Вспышка показывает, где произошло событие: дешёвая обратная связь, которую чувствуют сразу  ·  [02:56](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=176)
**Мысль:** Короткая вспышка объекта указывает, где произошло столкновение; такая обратная связь недорога, и игроки чувствуют её, даже не умея объяснить.

> Nothing about the gameplay changed, but the right side already reads better. The flash is doing real work. It tells your eyes exactly where the collision happened, a split second after it happened. That's feedback. The talk's point is that feedback like this is cheap to add and players feel it instantly, even if they could never tell you why.

**Для Напарника:** Короткое мягкое подсвечивание затронутого элемента (шаг, постройка) вместо анимации экрана.


### Кривая плавности задаёт характер: линейная механична, эластичная желейна  ·  [03:21](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=201)
**Мысль:** При той же длительности и концах кривая меняет личность: линейная как робот, ease-out мягче, elastic с перелётом как желе. Каскад с задержкой по ряду и колонке.

> Things popping into place, they move there over time. The magic is in the easing function, a curve that maps time from zero to one into a feeling. Here we use an elastic ease, the same family Robert Penner made famous. Each brick gets a small delay based on its row and column. So the wall cascades in. And when the ball hits anything, every brick bumps a little bigger, then settles back. Let's reset the level and see it. Same tween, three different easing curves. Linear first. It moves, but it feels mechanical like a robot. Now an ease out cubic, which starts fast and slows down gently. smoother, more natural, and now elastic. It overshoots and wobbles into place like the bricks are made of jelly. Same duration, same start, same end. The curve alone changes the personality of the whole game.

**Для Напарника:** Подбирать easing под характер Напарника (ease-out, лёгкое перерегулирование для радости), использовать каскад для появления элементов.


### Squash and stretch: растяжение читается как скорость, сплющивание как вес  ·  [04:15](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=255)
**Мысль:** Добавление растяжения и сплющивания превращает твёрдый камень в мягкое живое существо без других изменений; площадь сохраняют, добавляют пружинку при ударе.

> Idea three, squash and stretch. Borrowed straight from classic animation, a fastmoving ball stretches along its direction of travel and squashes when it slows. We measure the speed, rotate to face the velocity, and scale one axis up and the other down so the area stays the same. Then we add a tiny spring. On every hit, the ball wobbles and the paddle squashes like it absorbed the impact. Now drag the stretch amount up and watch the ball go from a rigid to something rubbery. Side by side, on the left, the ball is a perfect circle always. On the right, it leans into its motion. Your brain reads stretch as speed and squash as weight. The book game feel by Steve Swink tells a story about the game to blob. Adding squash and stretch to a ball made it go from feeling like solid stone to feeling soft and alive with no other change.

**Для Напарника:** Для лёгких касаний и реакций (не трогая MascotSvg): небольшая упругость при появлении элементов острова.


### Тряска пропорциональна силе удара, пауза ощущается, а не видна  ·  [05:20](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=320)
**Мысль:** Использовать trauma в квадрате, чтобы малые удары почти не двигали, а большие били; микропауза в 50 мс чувствуется. Слишком сильная тряска тошнотворна, хорошая ощущается больше, чем видна.

> The famous one, screen shake. Instead of a fixed shake, we use a trauma value. A trick from Squirrel Eisera's camera talk at GDC. Each hit adds trauma and trauma slowly drains away. The shake offset is trauma squared times a strength. So small hits barely move and big hits really kick. We also add hit stop from Jan William Nijman's talk the art of screen shake. Freeze the game for 50 milliseconds on every brick. You don't see the pause, you feel the impact. Now compare. The left side hits bricks politely. The right side hits them like it means it. Let's turn the strength right up. That's too much and it's honestly a little nauseating. Now back down to about 10. Both talks make the same point here. Screen shake is powerful, so use it with care and keep it short. A good shake is felt more than it's seen.

**Для Напарника:** Принцип для хаптики и эффектов: масштаб отклика пропорционален значимости события, короткий и умеренный.


### Разрушение вместо исчезновения: у каждого удара есть начало, середина и конец  ·  [07:14](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=434)
**Мысль:** Падающие, вращающиеся и затемнённые кирпичи делают удар тяжёлым и дают маленькую историю; затемнение сохраняет читаемость.

> A brick that gets hit simply vanishes. The talk went through several versions. Fade it out, make it fall, let the ball push it, make it spin, and finally make it darker so it doesn't blend with the live bricks. We'll do all of that in a few lines. When a brick breaks, we push a copy into a falling list, carrying some of the ball's velocity in a random spin. Gravity takes it from there. Compare the two. On the left, bricks just blink out of existence. On the right, every brick tumbles away, spinning, pushed in the direction it was hit. It makes the ball feel heavy, and it gives every hit a little story with a beginning, a middle, and an end. The darker color matters, too. It keeps the falling pieces readable, so you never confuse them with bricks that are still in play.

**Для Напарника:** Завершённый шаг может не просто исчезать, а с мягким уходом/превращением во что-то (искра -> часть острова).


### Эффекты работают в наслоении, но избыток прячет важное: дозируй и дай reduced motion  ·  [09:39](https://youtu.be/ws0x6Oyiza8?t=579)
**Мысль:** Ни один слой сам по себе не драматичен, их сумма превращает прототип в игру; при слишком большом juice важное теряется, нужен вариант без движения.

> No single step is dramatic on its own. It's the stacking that turns a prototype into something you want to keep playing. One last trick, a single juice dial that scales every effect at once. At zero, we're back to a clean, colorful game with no feedback at all. At 1.5, it's chaos with particles everywhere and the screen jumping around. A research survey on game field points out exactly this risk. Too much juice can hide what actually matters in the game, so tune it and give players a reduced motion option.

**Для Напарника:** Поддержка prefers-reduced-motion для анимаций острова и ограничение числа слоёв эффекта на финале.



# 🎬 Master the 3 Types of CRAZY Mobile UI Swipe Interactions — Kole Jain


### Почти никогда не линейный easing: кривая задаёт тон  ·  [00:33](https://youtu.be/14h1VnkQvIc?t=33)
**Мысль:** В жизни вещи ускоряются и замедляются; кривая с упругостью делает жест живым и передаёт тон: чёткий, игривый или медленный и плавный.

> Opening up the easing curve to give it some more bounce makes this feel much more natural, allowing the cards to carry more momentum. Easing and bounce are remarkably important to make lifelike interactions. Almost never do you want a linear ease. Things in real life speed up and slow down. And by playing with that curve, we transform animations. It also helps provide tone like snappy and performant, fun and springy, or slow and smooth.

**Для Напарника:** Подбор easing для острова и Фокуса под тёплый, медленный тон; линейное только для индикаторов времени.


### Переход продолжает жест: контент следует направлению свайпа  ·  [03:19](https://youtu.be/14h1VnkQvIc?t=199)
**Мысль:** Фон и новый контент выезжают по направлению свайпа; изображение, увеличиваясь, становится героем нового экрана, создавая непрерывность.

> Make the motion feel natural by following the direction of the swipe with a background and new content sliding up and in. This is a principle that we'll see in action in the next example, too. This is a simple onboarding flow, but instead of just a button to slide us over, we have a button that is clickable or swipeable. And by making the motion follow the direction of the swipe and then wrap around into the next slide, we make it super fluid. It might make more sense how this works if I zoom out and show you everything. We start with a circle that expands very large and move the content out. Then we swap the circles out and shrink that one back down and move the new content in. It's actually a pretty simple effect when you see it like this. Now, those are all well and good, but they're fairly complex, but even something similar like this is just so much better than sliding a new page in. This swipe creates continuity because we have this image actually zooming in to become the new hero image.

**Для Напарника:** Переходы между Домом, Фокусом и Миром: общий AppBackdrop уже даёт непрерывность; добавить сдвиг контента по направлению таб-перехода.



# 🎬 Mechanically Speaking: Shooting in First Person - [game array] — Mason C Miller


### Оружие должно быть доминантным в одних ситуациях и слабым в других  ·  [10:30](https://youtu.be/13iMO9s-q0c?t=630)
**Мысль:** Роли создают интересные решения; выбор «дробовик или винтовка» интереснее, чем «дробовик или слабый дробовик».

> So much of this comes down to two simple rules. A weapon can’t be dominant in all situations. But a weapon has to be dominant in some situations. These two rules create roles. And roles are the key to interesting decisions. Interesting decisions have to matter. And decisions will only matter if each weapon is unique in the context of its own game. The choice between a shotgun and an assault rifle is far more interesting than the choice between a shotgun and a shotgun that does less damage. To make weapons truly unique me need to make sure each one is differentiated by a set of variables that are statistically independent from one another. That they aren’t just less and more powerful versions of one another.

**Для Напарника:** Варианты длительностей и типов сессий в Фокусе делать различными по смыслу (короткий разгон, глубокий заход), а не просто больше и меньше.



# 🎬 My App Design Process: What Took Me 5 Years to Learn in 10 Minutes — Chris Raroque


### Тестируй UX на реальном устройстве и с реальными данными  ·  [01:36](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=96)
**Мысль:** Фигма показывает UI, но UX проверяется только на устройстве; красивые фейковые данные и картинки скрывают проблемы, которые вылезают на реальных данных.

> The reason I want to test it on a real device is I want to test the ux or the user experience figma is great to be able to see the UI and see how it looks but I found if you really want to test the ux you need to do it on a real device so you can actually simulate what it's going to be like in the real world for users and I try really hard to test it with real data because sometimes fake data makes everything look really good there are so many designs that I've seen that look amazing because they have really good high quality imag or specific texts that just fit the screen and look really good but then in production with real data and lower quality images it just doesn't look as good so I try to test it with my own data or as real data as possible just so I can catch some ux problems that the really good fake data was hiding.

**Для Напарника:** Это ровно §5.2 CLAUDE.md: проверять Дом/Фокус/Мир на реалистичных данных (9 стартов, а не 1) на реальном вьюпорте.


### Отбрасывать красивое, если оно не служит функции  ·  [04:18](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=258)
**Мысль:** Карточка с глубиной не добавляла смысла (глубина должна выделять); группировка по типу ломала порядок релевантности. Скучный классический список дал лучший UX.

> The first prototype was this concept where the results are inside of a card and the card looked really cool once I physically saw it here mocked up I realized it kind of added some unnecessary depth to the app usually depth is added to highlight or Elevate something for example if we added depth to the search bar it would make that more prominent in the UI but in this case the depth being added to the search results didn't really add anything in my opinion so I know we got this inspiration from this mockup from Airbnb but it makes a lot more sense for them because they have this map in the background so they kind of want to show this and let things float over it but since we just have a white screen in the back and we don't really have anything behind it felt a little bit unnecessary so we decided to not go with that option so another concept was grouping the results by type so if the matches are from the task title they'd be grouped here if they're from the notes they'll be grouped here that made a lot of sense and from a UI perspective it really did feel like this would make the design a little bit more clean but one of the issues with this when we thought about it a little more is the way that my backend and type sense which is the service I'm using for search they return the results and it's ranked by relevancy so the order actually matters if we group things I realize it would actually impact that order let me show you guys an example in the ungroup version it could appear as the second result however in the grouped version it appears as result number four so because there would be an actual impact on functionality tried not to go with the grouping option either and the last concept that we tried was a more traditional search list that you see in a bunch of different other apps it is a little bit more boring compared to the first two but I felt like it had the best ux out of all the three options.

**Для Напарника:** Перед добавлением глубины/свечения/группировки спрашивать, что это выделяет; привязка к принципу из §10 CLAUDE.md.


### Если не добавляет опыта, вырезать; заменить значимым элементом  ·  [06:40](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=400)
**Мысль:** Иконки типа совпадения игнорировались, их убрали ради снижения когнитивной нагрузки; чекбоксы выполнено/нет вернули воздух и неожиданно улучшили сканирование. Лишние индикаторы тоже вырезаны.

> The next thing I noticed was the icons on the left side which indicated what type of match the result hads for example this one showed there's a match on the note there's a match on the subtask it was really good in theory but when I was using it didn't seem that helpful I was kind of just ignoring the icons every time I ran a search so we decided to cut it because my philosophy is if something isn't adding to the experience it's best to just remove it so it reduces cognitive load on the users but then removing it the design felt super text heavy and I really loved how in these mockups there are icons on the left because it does break up the text a little bit and make it a little bit more breathable so we did try to brainstorm something to replace those icons luckily we realized we forgot to add the complete and incomplete indicators on the task like the literal checkbox that shows that a task was complete maybe we can just put those there and when we tried that I actually feel like the ux improved a little bit because it made searching for incomplete task a little bit easier it's just easier to scan and see oh these ones are incomplete so that was an unintended side effect that came out of that where I feel like the ux actually improved for some reason we just didn't think about doing this in the original design so that was a pretty cool thing that happened so on that note of looking for things to cut and simplifying the UI I was trying to look for more things that weren't that necessary for me or I was ignoring and one of the things I noticed is there's a couple of indicators on the task that I didn't feel like were relevant during the search process one example is this indicator on the task shows how much time was spent on the task this is really relevant on certain screens like the task list screen as I was searching and I was trying to put myself in the user shoes I felt like that task indicator wasn't that relevant to the search so for now we cut all the indicators except for label subtask and Note and I feel like that would cover most of what people are going to be looking for when conducting a search if people ask to bring back the indicators I'll reconsider it but for now we cut it just to keep things simple.

**Для Напарника:** Аудит Дома и Фокуса: убрать декор, который пользователь игнорирует; вместо него использовать смысловые элементы (статус шага).


### Мелкие детали: высота поля, делители, хаптика, авто-клавиатура  ·  [09:21](https://youtu.be/H0czeRyXPxA?t=561)
**Мысль:** Итерация идёт в мелочах: оттенок разделителей, вибро при очистке и выборе, автооткрытие клавиатуры на экране поиска.

> Was playing with the height and size of the search box trying to figure out what shade of gray the dividers between each of the results would be not covered in the figma but what haptics we should add here there's a little vibration or haptic when you clear the search box or when you click on a search result a little tweak where when the user navigates to the search page the keyboard automatically opens up.

**Для Напарника:** Автофокус допустим на Фокусе (одна цель входа) и не на Доме (§4); хаптику вешать на события, а не каждый тап.

# 🎬 Neuromarketing: Decoding the consumer mind | Shikher Chaudhary | TEDxMICA — TEDx Talks


### Задержка загрузки на 2–3 секунды даёт стресс как от фильма ужасов  ·  [16:18](https://youtu.be/m8XBkwLPgLQ?t=978)
**Мысль:** Буферизация повышает пульс на 44%; при таком стрессе эмоциональной вовлечённости не будет.

> And there's a horrible 4G reception in the area, and your ad starts buffering, and it doesn't load in the first place. What does that do? Well, we did a study for Ericsson to understand how does that actually stress work within the brain, and we realized that people tend to get very, very, very stressed. In fact, we found that even a slight delay in loading and buffering leads to about a 44% increase in heart rate, which is a massive, massive amount. And we found that even a 2 to 3-second delay that they're facing, a 2 to 3-second delay gives them the same amount of stress that they feel while they were watching a horror movie.

**Для Напарника:** Реплики кота и экраны должны грузиться быстро или показывать мягкий скелетон; задержки ответа ИИ маскировать в характере.



# 🎬 Perfect Typography Scale for UI Design (Exact Blueprint) | Figma Tutorial — Mizko


### Отдельные стили для лейблов и абзацев: у лейбла маленький line-height, у абзаца большой  ·  [02:26](https://youtu.be/hwjiExdMiNY?t=146)
**Мысль:** Лейбл это слово-два без нескольких слоёв, ему тесный интерлиньяж не мешает, а абзацу нужен больший, иначе текст сжимается.

> They generally will set up a type Style for paragraphs and we tend to use paragraphs for all sorts of text anything that's text we generally have paragraph Styles but the issue is that if I go over to a real world project that I just wrapped up you can see here we have a dashboard of videos that have been uploaded for this company called screen app which is sort of your own dashboard and then we also have another screen once you click onto a video we have a bit of a transcription of the video and the user can ask questions into the AI and it will pull cool insights out as you can see over here the summary which generally has a bunch of paragraphs of text this is meant to be a paragraph style because a paragraph is really just a combination of multiple sentences put together so if you don't have a large enough line height then your text becomes very tight so for example let me just change this over to the label style you can see that it actually becomes SS very tight and it doesn't look that good so you want to expand that a little bit so that's why I have different styles for paragraph text and I also have different styles for labels because as you can see over here this is a label where it talks about for showcases who was the user that uploaded this video and this is also a label to Showcase how long the video is this is also a label to Showcase how many people were in this video as well so as you can see if I directly select this text we are actually using labels so really to summarize the difference between a label and a paragraph is that a label is just a couple of words or even just one word and it generally doesn't need a large line height because we don't have multiple layers of labels on top of each other however with a paragraph because there's going to be multiple lines of text we need larger line height.

**Для Напарника:** В дизайн-системе Напарника завести разные типографические токены для коротких подписей и для реплик в чате.


### Реальный проект: база 16px, большинство текстов это лейблы; абзацы только для больших кусков  ·  [07:11](https://youtu.be/hwjiExdMiNY?t=431)
**Мысль:** Метаданные делаются мелкими (12px), заголовки и лейблы дают иерархию, а абзацный стиль нужен только для длинного текста.

> There was no magic pill that I took it really is using 16 pixels as a base and anything from there I would either increase it by 2 pixels or decrease it by two or four pixels so as you can see most of the labels which are generally should be labels so one words are generally labels not paragraphs you should only be using paragraph styles for large chunks of text it's generally going to be a medium because I personally like using 16 pixels as a base font because I generally like to use 16 pixels as a baseline for my projects because I think 16 pixels is quite easy to read you can see that we have a large label which is semi bold but then the meta data for this card which I wanted to create more visual hierarchy we are using the label small medium so generally as you can see small is going to be 12 pixels and anything else will be around 16 to 14 pixels now let's take a look at this screen over here we've got a model for actually sharing or inviting people to this collaboration folder once again we have a heading which is H4 and then we have paragraph which is medium that's 16 pixels then we have a label that's 16 pixels because it's medium and then everything else is also Medium as well you don't really need crazy type scales for your projects.

**Для Напарника:** На Фокусе почти всё это лейблы и заголовок шага; абзацный стиль оставить для реплик напарника.



# 🎬 Pixel Art Class - Simple Character Idle Animation — AdamCYounis


### Дыхание в два состояния: сдвиг верхней части на 1 пиксель, 10-12 кадров  ·  [04:49](https://youtu.be/vs-ierVdE7I?t=289)
**Мысль:** Простой idle строится из вдоха и выдоха: верх тела над бёдрами сдвигается на 1 пиксель, большинство кадров — удержание, а переход растягивают на несколько кадров для медленного вдоха.

> Let's say we want this to be just a simple Idol we're going between two major sort of points exhale inhale I'm going to take all of the stuff that's above the hips and I'm going to shift it down one pixel so these are my two you know States I suppose and I'm going to give 10 frames for this like most of this stuff is like a holding so we can do it this way and I do prefer to do it this way just in case I want to do some more subtle sub pixel animations during these pauses right like if you look at this we've got one up and one down over five frames so the approaches we can take is we can try to modulate those slowly over the course so it's a slow exhale slow inhale over 10 frames or we can do like two or three frames either side so it's like inhale over three frames exhale over three frames and everything in the middle can just be kind of like either like repeated frames or you can even extend the duration of these frames now for my game I don't have any way of interpreting these durations so for me I'd have to then go into the engine and duplicate the frames or tolerate them being duplicated here I'm going to try to like just keep it really simple and a little bit of waste of data it's not a big issue I might even go to 12 frames actually.

**Для Напарника:** Дыхание кота: медленный цикл (4-7 с) с сдвигом на 1-2 px/scale 1-2%, большая часть цикла в удержании.


### Сглаживай переходы: от центра к конечностям, промежуточный тон  ·  [06:21](https://youtu.be/vs-ierVdE7I?t=381)
**Мысль:** Чтобы дыхание не дёргалось, начинай с груди и распространяй к краям; промежуточные серые пиксели делают переход мягким вместо резкого скачка всего сразу.

> What I want to do is take this transition point and kind of smoothing it out a little bit in order to make this feel subtle right now it feels quite jarring because everything moves at once but I think what I can do is start with the core elements first and then animate kind of like extending outwards to the extremities so we can start with the chest and we can start bringing down parts of this so we could take maybe this part here and start descending that and start just smoothing a lot of this out and I might even bring some of this up and then feather out this so it's more of a sub pixel drop okay so this middle tone it's almost like the white is moving from white to gray to black or to the darker gray over three frames and it makes it feel a lot less a lot less jarring and we might even keep one of these here so it's this again feels like a transition because it's like three pixels moving to one pixel moving to no pixels on this line which also feels like a transition.

**Для Напарника:** Для SVG-кота анимировать части со сдвигом по фазе (корпус, затем хвост/уши), а не единым transform; мягкий opacity-переход.


### Вторичное движение с задержкой и мягкая посадка в покой  ·  [10:07](https://youtu.be/vs-ierVdE7I?t=607)
**Мысль:** Руки и волосы реагируют позже тела, а почти конечный кадр делают близким к финальному, чтобы не двигаться равномерно: «почти готово, потом готово» читается как отдых.

> I want to do a little more secondary motion so when we start to shift down here what I'm going to do is have the hands respond a little bit later so I'm going to instead of dropping them down I'm going to move them out and let's have that happen a little further up and I want that to be a little more like delayed I want that to take a few more frames so we're just going to see what we can get away with here so then on this Frame we're going to include a little bit more transition maybe even flare this out slightly more just to give us a little more something and the same for the arms maybe we could even slightly shift some of this shoulder pad stuff around and I think this Frame it should be like as close as we can get it to the SEC to the last frame cuz I don't want it to feel like we're moving evenly I want it to feel like we're moving so it's like out almost finished and then finished so this here is probably a little too high cramp this down a little so it's like just getting it as close as we can to that last frame I think that makes it feel more of like a more of like a rest.

**Для Напарника:** Задержка и мягкая посадка на ease-out для реакций кота и искр костра: быстро вышел, плавно осел.


### Idle как удержание + периодический «вдох» по таймеру  ·  [16:09](https://youtu.be/vs-ierVdE7I?t=969)
**Мысль:** Одно-кадровый idle держат произвольное время (1-2 с), затем проигрывают короткую анимацию дыхания и возвращаются, поэтому жизнь не повторяется механически.

> Frame one and then call this a new tag idle and now we can sit here and then we can play this and then we can sit back here again for as long as we want and we can have the character breathe you know periodically or whatever we want to do in the script my animation system allows me to play a onef frame animation for as long as I want so I could say hold this for 2 seconds or hold this for 1 second and then play a breathe and then go back to the start and do the same thing again so in my engine I could do it this way.

**Для Напарника:** Реализация кота: статичный кадр, и через случайные интервалы короткие жесты (моргание, вдох); без жёсткого цикла.



# 🎬 Pode, a Rose Painted Adventure — GDC Festival of Gaming


### Модульные ассеты: общий набор плюс уникальные детали на уровень  ·  [09:16](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=556)
**Мысль:** Небольшой набор универсальных элементов плюс специфичные для уровня растения, текстуры и цветовая схема создают впечатление гораздо большего разнообразия, чем есть на самом деле.

> The most important thing we did was to create modular world building and decorative assets we made a small set of generic assets for the entire game including walls floors filler plants and some decorative elements and from these we created multiple combinations some of them generic and some of them level specific then we had a small set of level specific plant assets to give each level its distinct look mushrooms and pine cones for the pine woods level and cloudberries and cotton grass for the birch woods and so on and so we did the same with textures we had some generic textures with a few variations and level specific textures with different colors and patterns and this approach makes each level feel new in spite of the reuse of assets unique floral and crystal combinations give each level the feel of its own shape and texture the combination of generic assets and level specific assets give the impression of a much bigger variety than there actually is.

**Для Напарника:** Для спрайтов острова (island-sprites): общий словарь форм, различающийся палитрой и парой уникальных деталей на каждый ориентир.


### Малые комнаты разного размера избегают повторения и дают чувство эволюции  ·  [10:23](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=623)
**Мысль:** Маленькое пространство даёт меньше повторов; новые растения по мере продвижения создают ощущение, что мир постоянно меняется.

> Another thing we did was to make small playable areas there are nine different levels in the game all based on different parts of norwegian nature and each level is split into smaller rooms varying the size of the rooms prevents the feeling of repetition so going from a tiny room to a more open one shifting from moving in depth to moving horizontally or vertically completely closed in rooms versus open area ones always keeping the navigational space relatively small but giving the illusion of big open bottomless spaces sorry the small the small size means there is less repetition in use of different plants and combinations so we could create variation in flora not only from level to level but also from room to room introducing new level specific plants and crystals and combinations the further into a level you go makes it feel like the world is constantly evolving throughout the game.

**Для Напарника:** Каждая сессия может показывать небольшой новый элемент, а не крупные редкие изменения: постоянное ощущение развития.


### Рост растения: после роста замени динамику на жёсткий объект с лёгким покачиванием  ·  [13:21](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=801)
**Мысль:** Морфинг нужен лишь для нескольких вещей; в сочетании со scale и rotation получается иллюзия органического роста, а после роста объект становится жёстким с лёгким вращением, что резко снижает нагрузку.

> We started out with a lot of morphing objects but luckily we discovered that this was only necessary for a few things and combined with scale and rotation that it gave the impression of natural growth so for this the amount of objects actually worked in our favor which was nice for a change and combined with the speed of growth it helped the illusion as you never really focus on one single object we use the same approach for idle animation where after our plant has grown we switched it from a dynamic object to a rigid object with slight rotation just to give the impression of constant movement in life and this had a massive impact on latency.

**Для Напарника:** Для анимации роста ориентиров: scale+rotate плюс лёгкое покачивание вместо тяжёлых морфингов, особенно на мобильном; соответствует медленным циклам фона.


### Каскадное цветение вместо ручного запуска каждого объекта  ·  [14:06](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=846)
**Мысль:** Отдельный запуск роста каждого растения не работал на больших скоплениях. Несколько триггеров запускают последовательный каскад с контролируемыми паузами, а трава растёт чуть медленнее движения игрока: ощущение свободы при сохранении контроля у разработчика.

> For plant and crystal clusters we started out with everything being separately triggered to grow we figured we wanted we wanted people to move around and kind of just like make everything grow as they went but we kind of discovered that this wouldn't work with large collections of plants and we wanted we wanted control of how the combinations grew and looked and for it to be less of a hassle to for the player to grow everything in a room so we decided on using a few triggers spread out through each level to set off a cascading bloom and we created a tool to set up our growing objects in sequence this also let us control the timing between each step in a sequence so everything could be perfectly timed the grass is a combination of single triggers and sequencing to give the player a bit more freedom so if you trigger the grass and stay still it'll keep growing but the spread is slower than the character's movement so if you move around while using the glow ability you'll get to paint it in yourself all these things combined gives the player the impression of full freedom to grow the world but with us having enough control so the world feels cohesive and everything grows with a nice flow.

**Для Напарника:** Появление новой постройки или награды на острове как каскад (последовательность с задержками), а не всё одновременно: хореография пика дофамина.


### Урок малой команды: мало ассетов, безжалостный отсев, визуал служит истории  ·  [15:37](https://youtu.be/lv3YqqWs0qU?t=937)
**Мысль:** Мало переиспользуемых ассетов, постоянно резать всё, что не нужно для цели, строить игру мачете. Визуал - инструмент рассказа: растущий мир отражает растущую дружбу.

> From this is you need to work smart streamline your pipeline because with a small team there is absolutely no time for idleness make few and reusable assets you really just need enough variation not to hinder the creative process or make the game feel repetitive you need to be constantly cutting everything not vital to your core goals i mean the phrase kill your darlings is cliche for a reason right you simply can't create quality content if you don't have the time or resources to do so you have to be ruthless build your game with a machete focus on what's most important and perfect that you have to make sure not to lose track of your goals visual design is really just a tool to help tell a story and for us it's a growing evolving world telling the story of a growing friendship and evolving emotions our increasingly detailed and beautiful world mirrors how empathy selflessness and cooperation makes the world more beautiful.

**Для Напарника:** Остров и кот как визуальная метафора растущих отношений и присутствия, а не декор; при ограниченных ресурсах ресурсы на главную сцену.



# 🎬 Psychology of Website Design: Neuromarketing and Lead Generation — Orbit Media Studios


### Самое заметное должно быть самым ценным  ·  [11:22](https://youtu.be/vVp6UpQb65U?t=682)
**Мысль:** Крупный подзаголовок бессмысленный, а ценные отзывы спрятаны в карусели и маленьком тексте; проверка иерархии — «важнейшее ли то, что виднее всего?».

> Let's look at this example what our company what our customers are saying huh the top of the visual hierarchy here says nothing super vague useless not helpful so and worse than that all the test is a testimonial block all the testimonials are inside a carousel bummer not helpful right you've got they're hidden so the big subhead is vague the carousel hides the valuable testimonials until they get clicked right they should have just broken them out and stacked them all up and then the tiny text is actually the best thing on the page basically we need to ask ourselves is the most visually prominent thing here also the most valuable compelling specific helpful thing here that's web design that's a landing a page with visual with visitor psychology is the most visually prominent thing also the most important thing if no you made a mistake go fix your website it's not that hard.

**Для Напарника:** На каждом экране Напарника главный по размеру элемент — главное действие; не прятать ценное в каруселях.



# 🎬 Scaling Taste, Craft & Quality in the AI Era | Figma x Mistral | RAISE Summit 2026 — RAISE Summit


### ИИ поднял пол, но потолок остаётся за человеком  ·  [07:13](https://youtu.be/IdaYjarEJFI?t=433)
**Мысль:** Легко дойти до 5-6 из 10 быстрым дофаминовым попаданием, но довести до 10 может только человек, потому что модель смотрит в прошлое по данным; нужно двигать вперёд культуру.

> But you know you have this very powerful magic wand and you know what can feel like this fast dopamine hit of I can see something. it's easy to get to a five or a six out of 10, but it's actually very hard to raise to that 10 out of 10 level of quality. And so I think I think Dylan Field, Figma CEO, actually recently talked about this where he said AI had lowered the floor but hasn't yet raised the ceiling. You are responsible for raising the ceiling. do you have a take on what Dylan meant by this? >> I sure do. so and he said this at the conference with a bit of an apology in some ways saying like you know I came here and I told you AI will raise the floor but it also raised the ceiling and the reality if you look back for sure AI has raised the floor in absolutely every discipline. We could do so much more. but the more you use AI, the more you realize that the job for raising the ceiling remains with the human. And the reason why that is simply because of how AI is built, AI is built on data that humans have already produced. And by definition, it kind of looks back at the past. but if there's one thing about humans that we know is that we don't settle, we want change, we want to do the next thing. And so our job is to push culture for forward. If you think about design, what was cool two years ago is no longer cool. And if a model is trained on the corpus of data that you know stopped six months ago, it's already out of date in terms of what it could produce for you. So this is what we mean about you know you raise the ceiling. I think we need to continue to push our disciplines forward whether it's engineering or design or PM.

**Для Напарника:** Не полагаться на ИИ-генерацию как на финал: реплики напарника и визуалы доводить вручную до уровня «кто-то это обдумал».


### Ребрендинг Mistral: 11-12 недель вместо 4 ради ручной проработки  ·  [10:36](https://youtu.be/IdaYjarEJFI?t=636)
**Мысль:** Против холодного «чистого» вида ИИ-брендов команда выбрала оптимизм: палитра восхода, ручные иллюстрации, мелкие детали; суммарно это можно ощутить, и это отличается от «достаточно хорошо».

> We recently as we just did a recent brand refresh where craft >> it was really cool. >> Thank you. Where craft was super important. basically we went from you know we're only three years old. We went from three genius scientists and are now a thousand person global AI full stack company and we wanted to reflect that growth with this development of the brand. But we also are very aware of, you know, the fear and uncertainty in the air. And there's this sense that some of the AI companies and branding out there is this very clean but cold feeling. And that can be weird. Like sometimes people think of this dystopian kind of future that is already here, but it's not. like we as humans and designers and builders have a strong active choice in how we shape that future. And so we kept landing on this idea of optimism and how do we how do we show that optimism through craft because it's one thing to say that but visually speaking that craft we could have easily stopped this brand work after 4 weeks but we pushed it for 11 12 weeks because we really wanted to bring this colorful sunrise palette to contrast sterile white and black kind of feel these somber tones. All of our illustrations are very fun and they're actually handdrawn by our wonderful brand designer. we have these beautiful little scientific details even that kind of point to our science roots. and so and we worked with the wonderful folks at Metal Lab to help bring this to life too. And at the end of the day, all of these little details added up to something that you really can feel. and this is different than just what was good enough. And so, I think that was really important and we could have easily stopped, but we felt like moving slowly in the right kind of handcrafted direction felt better than landing on something kind of good enough quickly.

**Для Напарника:** Подтверждает тёплую, рукотворную эстетику Напарника (кот, остров) против стерильного вида; детали складываются в ощущение.



# 🎬 Simple 2D Idle Animation for your Character | Handdrawn | Beginner 2D Game Art — Nonsensical 2D


### Лицо в idle лучше держать статичным, если тело дышит  ·  [08:12](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=492)
**Мысль:** Одновременное покачивание, улыбка и моргание каждые доли секунды выглядят неловко и снижают погружение. Автор считает лучше оставить лицо стабильным.

> I will generally keep the face consistent throughout the entire animation we can see this in my current Running Animation these flaps swing back and forth but the face is static and we can see this in Hollow Knight as well my biggest reason for keeping the head and facial movement consistent throughout animation is that I generally feel that those types of Animation can reduce immersion if I were to sit up and bob up and down with each breath looks somewhat natural but if I were to bob up and down and smile and blink every 10th of a second it would feel kind of awkward and so in my opinion and hopefully yours too tends to be better and just keep it static and not open up for those type of awkward looks so what I'll do.

**Для Напарника:** Для кота: не накладывать частое моргание на дыхание; редкие выражения только по событиям (согласуется с контингентностью). Противоречит советам первого ролика про живое лицо, решать по контексту.


### Начни с двух крайних поз, чтобы ограничить амплитуду  ·  [09:08](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=548)
**Мысль:** Две ключевые позы задают диапазон движения заранее, анимация живёт между ними.

> The reason I start with the head is that it helps me figure out how much my character is going to move overall and it also helps me contain the animation so now I know that my animation is going to exist between these two frames and the jump is not going to be bigger or smaller than this.

**Для Напарника:** Для CSS-дыхания кота зафиксировать два состояния (scale/translate) и анимировать только между ними.


### Минимум кадров и ping-pong вместо loop для idle  ·  [10:46](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=646)
**Мысль:** Добавлять кадры только до исчезновения рваности; ping-pong для idle вдвое сокращает работу, но не подходит для бега.

> I don't generally care too much about whether I have 4 or 24 frames I tend to just add the least amount of frames I feel are necessary to get the animation the way I want it to be what this means is I'll slow it down to the type of speed I wanted to move and then I'll check if it's to jacket and if it's too Jagged then I'll add more frames when I'm shaking my animation I have it set to ping pong the animation so in most animation software you have it to try the ping pong or Loop what Ping Pong does is that it makes the animation play back and forth rather than repeat itself so the ping pong goes back and forth whereas the loop goes whoop and starts over Roop so we'll go to my last frame and then cycle all the way back to the start this kind of makes sense for an idle animation and makes it so that we only have to animate half as much.

**Для Напарника:** В CSS дыхание кота делать animation-direction: alternate с easing, а не два отдельных кейфрейма вверх и вниз.


### Вторичные части (ткань, уши, хвост) идут с запаздыванием  ·  [11:41](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=701)
**Мысль:** Свободные части имеют инерцию и отстают от основного движения, поэтому их анимируют после основного и не синхронизируют.

> When I have my main movement laid out I'll add the small Fabric and Flappy bits the reason I do them after my main movement is that these will generally be out of sync from my main animation which makes sense if you think about it they have some inertia and they aren't attached to me so when I move quickly it will take some time for it to catch up so it'll be easier to just think about and consider how these Flappy bits fly.

**Для Напарника:** Хвост и усы кота анимировать со сдвигом фазы относительно дыхания тела.


### Лёгкая неидеальность читается как стиль, если выдерживать последовательно  ·  [15:19](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=919)
**Мысль:** Если всё чистое, любой недочёт выглядит ошибкой; если неидеальность везде, она воспринимается как намеренная.

> Making everything too clean will tell the viewer that I want Merit to be clean and then every time something is left unpolished the viewer will see that as a mistake but if I just leaving small mistakes or a wavy line art constantly they'll see it and go well he obviously knows what he's doing so he wants it there it's intentional.

**Для Напарника:** Рукотворные неровности иллюстраций острова и кота допустимы, если выдержаны во всём продукте.


### Дай работе отлежаться: дистанция показывает реальные проблемы  ·  [17:06](https://youtu.be/RlS-6mPcoak?t=1026)
**Мысль:** Время вдали от проекта даёт перспективу, что действительно является проблемой; перфекционизм мешает закончить.

> But the thing is I'm never fully happy with any of darts I do but after work done it then give it into a try I let it sit and then I come back to it in the future because time away from the project will give me perspective on whether it is an issue or not as an example when I made my staging scene I tried focusing on the core idea of where you might place your main background assets to maximize Paradox but then I completely overlooked working on the background so it looked really flat and empty but eventually this bugged me a lot so before I started my video on environment animations I went back into the scene and fixed it and we can see if I compared it to the difference it made and that tends to be the process I work through I don't like the idea of trying to perfect things because you would simply never get things done.

**Для Напарника:** Процессный совет: полировку экранов делать итерациями с паузой, а не доводить до идеала за раз.



# 🎬 Sir Jony Ive's 2021 CCA Commencement Honorary Doctorate Address — California College of the Arts - CCA


### Уважать свои идеи и процесс всегда, не только когда они хороши  ·  [08:19](https://youtu.be/gr3GKcwG9s8?t=499)
**Мысль:** Привычка относиться к идеям с уважением повышает вероятность того, что они станут достойными этого уважения.

> I came to learn from him that creating and thinking should always be afforded a rare respect. And this is the important thing. Always a rare respect, not only when the ideas are good, and not only if the circumstances are easy and convenient. If we make it our habit to respect our ideas and our process, we increase the probability that they will actually be good and worthy of that respect.


### Навыки растут от повторения, креативность от заботы и намерения  ·  [09:04](https://youtu.be/gr3GKcwG9s8?t=544)
**Мысль:** Профессиональные навыки перехватывают внимание, поэтому о глубинной креативной части нужно заботиться осознанно.

> To be neither distracted nor limited by your respective areas of expertise. Of course, your disciplines and the skills you have learned are wonderfully important, but just know they will tend to naturally win the competition for your attention. My encouragement is to be committed and proactive in caring for that deep creative part of yourself that is easily overlooked. To develop an unconditional respect for your creativity, which will sustain you during the times when the ideas are either sparse or not very good. Our professional skills develop with repetition. Our creativity develops with deep care and intention.


### Идеи хрупки: не начинать с критики проблем, а удерживать саму идею  ·  [10:04](https://youtu.be/gr3GKcwG9s8?t=604)
**Мысль:** Новая идея частична и недоказана; если сразу сосредоточиться на известных проблемах, теряется вера в решение. Критика без конструктивной идеи разрушительна.

> I obsess trying to deeply understand the general nature of ideas. Understanding their nature, I tend to have more ideas and do a better job caring for, protecting, and developing them. Ideas, by definition, are always fragile. If they were resolved, they wouldn't be ideas. They would be products that were ready to ship. I've come to learn that you have to make an extraordinary effort not to focus on the problems which are implicated with any new idea. These problems are known. They're quantifiable and understood.
